# math-research

Machine-checked Lean 4 formalizations of exact determinant factorizations, binary-arithmetic dynamics, spectral graph counts, and combinatorial inequalities developed at MoteMath.

Each subdirectory is a self-contained Lake project with its own toolchain pin, `README.md` (with a **Synthetic Score**, **For Mathematicians** summary, and **For Non-Mathematicians** overview), `AUDIT.md`, and build instructions. Projects follow the [Palomar](https://palomar-registry.org/) submission layout (`Challenge.lean`, `Solution.lean`, `comparator.json`, `formalization.yaml`).

## Synthetic Score Matrix

Each project carries a heuristic self-rating out of `100` across five `1–10` dimensions (not a substitute for peer review):

```math
\mathrm{Score} = 3\cdot\mathrm{Nov} + 2\cdot\mathrm{Sur} + 2\cdot\mathrm{Gen} + 2\cdot\mathrm{Use} + 1\cdot\mathrm{Ver}.
```

- **Nov (30%):** Novelty — distance from published literature and OEIS.
- **Sur (20%):** Surprise & Depth — non-obviousness of the collapse or proof mechanism.
- **Gen (20%):** Generality — arbitrary rings/radices, all sizes/widths, exact `iff` laws.
- **Use (20%):** Usefulness — bridge value to bit-level arithmetic, graphs, or combinatorics.
- **Ver (10%):** Verification — `10` for pure `Init`/`Std` (zero Mathlib) + Palomar `nanoda`; `9` for Mathlib + Palomar `nanoda` (all zero `sorry`, standard axioms only).

## Findings

### 1. [`mxfp4-nvfp4-opt-clipping/`](mxfp4-nvfp4-opt-clipping/) — **97 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Proves exact clipping laws for a hand-written nearest-grid model of 4-bit `MXFP4` and Blackwell `NVFP4` block quantization (not the OCP/NVIDIA hardware formats): intentionally dropping a block's power-of-two `E8M0` scale by 1 step cuts worst-case MSE by **`-38.4%`** at `B = 32` (`47/431` vs `31/175`), proves that, under stated hypotheses on `M` and `|u|`, dropping two exponents never helps in the model (a `1-bit` check suffices), proves the modelled optimal `E4M3` block scaling for `NVFP4` cuts error to `23/887` at `B = 16` (`1/625` at `B = 1` via `s = 13/8` synergy!), and proves repurposing `E2M1`'s redundant `-0.0` bit pattern to `c = 1/4` cuts `MXFP4` block error nearly in half (`95/1631`) with zero extra bits.
- **For Mathematicians:** In the hand-written model of `Defs.lean`: exact piecewise-linear `E8M0` scale-down gain $\Phi(M) \in \{0, 2M - 7, 6M - 27\}$ and universal block `iff`; universal all-$B$ 3-regime optimal-`E8M0` EF21 contraction law via $\lambda _ 0 = \frac{3}{4}(1 - \omega)$ with tight witnesses (`47/431` at $B = 32$, `23/151` at $B = 64$, `1/5` at $B = 145$); exact all-$B$ `NVFP4` ceil-vs-nearest `E4M3` EF21 laws (`199/4231` vs `151/4759` at $B = 16$, unrestricted $u = 5$ transitions at $B = 12, 20$); optimal `NVFP4` `E4M3` certificates (`23/887` at $B = 16$, `1/625` at $B = 1$); never-drop-two-exponents inequality (under stated hypotheses on $M$ and $|u|$) $(3 - 6s)(2M - 3 - 6s) \ge 3M - 27/4 > 9/4$; and exact `-0.0` repurposing laws ($c = 8 \to 47/431$, $c = 1/4 \to 95/1631$ with $B = 33, 49$ transitions, $c = 5 \to 1/49$ at $B = 1$).

Then certifies two dyadic quintics found by a GPU search (optimality is not proved): **`(2, -9/8, 1/8)` hits exact `0` error in 5 steps across all 28 FP8-E4M3FN values in `[0.05, 1]`**, and **`(35/16, -31/16, 3/4)` confines all 564 BF16 values within 1 ULP (`1/128`, `44×–46×` smaller error than Jordan's in the same model)**.

### 15. [`grpo-k3-kl-horizons/`](grpo-k3-kl-horizons/) — **95 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Proves four numerical failure statements about hand-written models of **GRPO / PPO** training computations: Schulman's `k3` KL estimator, non-negative over the rationals, turns **negative in a BF16/FP16 rounding model** under two evaluation orders (`0x3b01 → -1/256`); in the model, subtracting quantized log-probabilities causes **clip-window collapse (`0` interior ratios)** once $|\log p| \ge 2$ in FP8-E4M3 or $|\log p| \ge 32$ in BF16 (cured by direct logit-difference subtraction); binary-reward GRPO over-weights `1/G` extreme prompts by **`(G - 1)×` in squared advantage** (the difficulty bias is Dr.GRPO's finding; this is its exact closed form); and naive one-pass BF16 variance goes **strictly negative (`-1/8 < 0`)** while FP8-E4M3 `G = 64` mean saturates (`64/64 = 1 ≠ 4`).

- **Prior art & stack:** Builds on Schulman (2020), DeepSeekMath / GRPO (Shao et al. 2024), DeepSeek-R1 (2025), and Dr.GRPO (Liu et al. 2025); negative `k3` values from cancellation in low precision are already reported publicly ([google/tunix issue 2315](https://github.com/google/tunix/issues/2315); [unslothai/unsloth-zoo PR 1418](https://github.com/unslothai/unsloth-zoo/pull/1418); [arXiv:2611.30835](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30835) discusses negative KL estimates in low precision in general, abstract-level reading only), and the difficulty bias is the finding of Dr.GRPO ([arXiv:2503.20783](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20783)); the package adds machine-checked RNE instances for the model (the exact bit patterns were not found elsewhere by the authors; the search was not recorded). ### 24. [`relu2-teal-deadzone-bounds/`](relu2-teal-deadzone-bounds/) — **95 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Proves, from closed-form error formulas (`preMaxErr`, `postMaxErr`), a **Uniform vs Floating-Point Quantization-Order Split** for `ReLU^2` LLMs: for uniform `INT4`, quantizing **before** squaring (`Q(ReLU(x))^2`) beats quantizing after squaring (shrinking the zero-underflow deadzone `> 5×`), whereas for floating-point (`MXFP4`, `NVFP4`, `FP8-E4M3`), quantizing **after** squaring beats pre-square by exact **`4×` (`+6.02 dB`)** because squaring doubles relative mantissa error! Also proves **`TEAL`/`CATS` shifted deadzone reclamation** cuts the modelled uniform MSE by $(1 - \tau/M)^{-2}$ at zero extra bits and has a **super-quadratic floating-point step contraction** (`> (1 - \tau/M)^{-2}`, up to `28×` on `NVFP4` in the model), proves `ReLU^2` kurtosis `70/3` as a moment identity over finite weighted point sets (weights not required nonnegative; no witness distribution), and shows that pruning before quantization avoids the mask flips in the model.
- **For Mathematicians:** Exact rational moment proof that half-rectified `ReLU` has kurtosis `6` while `ReLU^2` has exact kurtosis `70/3` (and elevates outlier weight by $c^4$: $p c^4 \to p c^8$); exact uniform-quantizer `POST`-vs-`PRE` deadzone ratio $2N$ and strict `PRE` dominance for $x < M(1/2 - 1/(4N))$; exact floating-point `PRE`-vs-`POST` two-sided inflation bounds $(2 - \varepsilon)^2 \le \mathrm{ratio} \le (2 + \varepsilon)^2 \to 4\times$; exact uniform `TEAL`/`CATS` shifted-deadzone MSE cut $(1 - \tau/M)^{-2}$ (`4×, 16×, 64×` at $\tau/M = 1/2, 3/4, 7/8$); exact super-quadratic floating-point step contraction $\frac{x - \tau}{x}\cdot\frac{M}{M - \tau} < 1$ on $(\tau, M)$; and exact pre-pruning mask-flip band $[\tau - \Delta/2, \tau + \Delta/2)$ plus `uint64_t` bitmask sparse `W4` traffic-model crossover $s^* = \mathrm{ov}/(1 + \mathrm{ov}) < 1/5$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Builds on `ReLU^2` (So et al. 2021), `TEAL` (Liu et al. 2024), and `CATS` (Lee et al. 2024); an external audit (`NOVELTY.md`, 2026-11-27; shallow search) located related work on `ReLU^2` sparsity ([arXiv:2402.03804](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03804)) and BitNet a4.8, and no prior statement of the exact kurtosis value `70/3`; this README does not claim a recorded literature search. Lean `v4.34.1` / pure `Init` (`propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`).

### 25. [`ssm-tustin-pade-horizons/`](ssm-tustin-pade-horizons/) — **95 / 100**

Proves subtraction-free **`Padé-(0,2)`** ($A = 1/(1 + z + z^2/2)$, $B = 1/(1 + z/2 + z^2/12)$) guarantees **`0%` blackout**, **`0` negative/unstable poles**, **4th-order accuracy (`19×` tighter than Tustin at $z = 1$)**, and uniform trajectory stability for all $T$.

### 28. [`silicon-circuit-bisim-compiler/`](silicon-circuit-bisim-compiler/) — **95 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Real computer chips rarely use the slow textbook ripple-carry adder or naive shifter: they use intricate recursive parallel trees like Sklansky, Brent–Kung, and Kogge–Stone adders, logarithmic barrel rotators, Omega butterfly networks, and Batcher sorting networks built from raw NAND/XOR/MUX gates. Instead of writing a separate mathematical proof for every gate layout, we built a verified Lean 4 compiler that checks a tiny finite binary certificate on the building-block gate cell and automatically proves that the entire parallel circuit is bit-for-bit identical to the simple reference specification for **every** word width ($8, 32, 64, 2^{100}$ bits) — even for clever "glitched" hardware cells that only work on reachable carry states. The circuits are the authors' own recursive definitions in a small gate-level language in the package, not netlists from a chip library.
- **For Mathematicians:** We build **`SiliconBisim`**, a four-engine, kernel-verified circuit-generator bisimulation compiler over an explicit first-order Boolean gate-DAG DSL (`GateNet`). Rather than proving each hardware family equivalent by hand, each engine accepts concrete gate netlists plus a pure-data finite binary certificate (`BitVec` relation masks, reachable-token masks, or finite truth-table checks, zero `Prop` fields), checks it via kernel `decide` (zero `native_decide`), and lifts it to universally quantified equivalence across all widths ($\forall w \in \mathbb{N}$ or $\forall k \in \mathbb{N}$ at width $2^k$):

1. **Engine 1 (`slice_bisim_sound`, `block2_bisim_sound`):** Arbitrary-state-width linear bisimulation and 2-bit speculative block synchronization, certifying ripple-carry $\equiv$ dual-rail complementary-carry and ripple-carry $\equiv$ 2-bit carry-select adders for all widths.
2. **Engine 2 (`prefix_all_topologies_sound`, `four_way_prefix_adder_equiv`, `parallel_gray_decoder_equiv`):** Reachable-subset associativity + monoid-action homomorphism certifies **Serial, Sklansky, Brent–Kung, and Kogge–Stone** topologies simultaneously — including don't-care-optimized glitched cells that fail global associativity on $\mathrm{BitVec}\; 2$ yet are sound on the reachable 3-state XOR-propagate subsemigroup.
3. **Engine 3 (`three_way_shifter_equiv`):** Gate-level 8-case mux reflection certifies recursive tree rotators $\equiv$ logarithmic barrel rotators $\equiv$ unary iterated rotators across differing gate netlists (SOP, NAND-only, XOR-AND) and arbitrary non-power-of-two widths.
4. **Engine 4 (`reed_muller_anf_butterfly_all_k`, `gf4_butterfly_inverse_all_k`, `bitonic_andOr_eq_oddEven_nand_all_k`):** 4-wire interchange lifting, 2-wire inverse cancellation, unconditional Pease/Omega constant-geometry equivalence, and Batcher Bitonic $\equiv$ Batcher Odd-Even $\equiv$ Threshold Sort. (A kernel-checked Knuth 0–1 Principle, `zero_one_principle`, is in `Theorems.lean`, not in `Challenge.lean`.) The circuits are the authors' own recursive definitions over the `GateNet` DSL; the theorems are about those definitions.
- **Prior art & stack:** Sklansky (1960), Pease (1968), Batcher (1968), Kogge–Stone (1973), Knuth (1973), Brent–Kung (1982); claimed contribution (not independently reviewed; no prior record was found by the authors and the search was not recorded): a unified 4-engine gate-DAG bisimulation compiler with reachable-subset associativity and a 256-cell butterfly taxonomy.

### 29. [`morton-carry-monodromy-renorm/`](morton-carry-monodromy-renorm/) — **94 / 100**
- **Plain English:** In 2D/3D graphics and spatial databases, coordinates are interleaved into a single Morton (Z-order) binary number. Take a $d$-dimensional Morton number of any precision $n$, cyclically rotate the $d$ coordinates, and add $1$ modulo $2^{dn}$. Even though adding $1$ ripples carries across every digit block, we prove that starting from $0$ always traces a crystal-clear fractal "Flag-Helix" cycle of exact length $(d+1)^n$ — for every dimension $d$ and every word width $n$. The same finite-automaton renormalization engine also classifies every single cycle in 2D, discovers a 4D coordinate twist that visits all $16^n$ states in one giant cycle, and exhibits a "Delayed-Fracture Trap" that looks full-period at widths $3$ and $6$ bits before snapping in half at $9$ bits.
- **For Mathematicians:** For any radix $B \ge 2$ and digit-level bijection $\pi \in \mathrm{Sym}(\mathrm{Fin}\, B)$ lifted digit-wise to $\Pi _ n : \mathrm{Fin}(B^n) \to \mathrm{Fin}(B^n)$, consider the carry-coupled map $F _ {\pi, n}(z) = (\Pi _ n(z) + 1) \bmod B^n$. Because $\pi$ scrambles bits within each $d$-bit Morton digit ($B = 2^d$), $F _ {\pi, n}$ is **not** a bit-triangular T-function. We formalize **Carry-Monodromy Renormalization (CMR)**: on the unique carry-emitting cycle $C^* _ \pi$ of $\tau _ \pi(a) = (\pi(a)+1) \bmod B$ (length $L^*(\pi)$), the $L^*(\pi)$-step Poincaré return on the fiber $\{0\} \times \mathrm{Fin}(B^{n-1})$ is conjugate to $F _ {\pi^{L^*(\pi)}, n-1}$. Iterating $\mathcal{R}(\pi) = \pi^{L^*(\pi)}$ inside the finite cyclic group $\langle \pi \rangle$ yields a finite `RenormAutomaton B S` whose orbit product gives the exact minimal period of $0$ for all $n \in \mathbb{N}$ (`cmr_full_period_compiler`).

This single compiler discharges four infinite theorem families:
1. **All-dimension $d$-D Morton Flag-Helix (`mortonRot_exact_period_all_d_n`, `mortonRot_orbit_iff_flagHelix`):** For all $d \ge 1$ and $n \ge 0$, cyclic coordinate rotation $+1$ on `BitVec (d * n)` has exact minimal period $(d+1)^n$ at $0$, and $z < (2^d)^n$ lies on this giant orbit iff it satisfies the base-$(d+1)$ Flag-Helix predicate `isFlagHelix`.
2. **Complete 2D Morton Swap Spectrum (`morton2D_exact_period_all_z`, `morton2D_period_dichotomy`):** Every state $z < 4^n$ has exact period `period2D n z`, strictly of the form $3^k$ ($k \le n$) or $2 \cdot 3^k$ ($k+2 \le n$), with closed-form cycle populations.
3. **Non-nilpotent 4D Full-Periodicity & 3D Trichotomy:** A pure 4D coordinate 3-cycle ($3 \nmid 16$) yields a single Hamiltonian cycle of length $16^n$ for all $n \ge 0$, while 3D coordinate permutations realize all three regimes ($4^n$, $8^n$, and $6 \cdot 8^{n-1}$).
4. **Delayed-Fracture Trap (`delayed_fracture_exact_period`, `delayed_fracture_closed_form`):** The 3-bit permutation $\pi _ {\mathrm{delay}}$ is full-period at $n = 1$ ($8$) and $n = 2$ ($64$), yet fractures for the first time at $n = 3$ ($256 < 512$), obeying $\mathrm{per}(0) \cdot 2^{\lfloor n/3 \rfloor} = 8^n$ for all $n \ge 0$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Outside bit-triangular T-functions (Klimov–Shamir, Anashin); adjacent to Nekrashevych (2005) rooted-tree odometers/wreath recursions; the CMR finite-automaton compiler, all-d Flag-Helix, 2D swap spectrum, 4D non-nilpotent full-periodicity and Delayed-Fracture Trap are the authors' own results, not independently reviewed; no prior record was found by the authors (the search was not recorded).

### 39. [`carry-presburger-skolem-compiler/`](carry-presburger-skolem-compiler/) — **93 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Standard automated arithmetic solvers like Lean's `omega` can reason about linear inequalities ($3y \le 5x + 5$), but break down the moment you mix them with bitwise operations (`&`, `^`), binary carries, and popcounts. We build a self-verifying Lean 4 proof compiler that peels off numbers one bit at a time from the least significant bit, tracks finite carry-and-remainder states, and even synthesizes an executable program that constructs the witness $y$ for any input $x$. Using this compiler, we prove a phase transition for adding a number $y$ to $x$ with at most $K$ binary carries to hit a target remainder mod $3$: allowing $1$ carry makes remainder $2$ four times easier, while allowing $2$ carries makes remainders $0$ and $1$ easier than remainder $2$ again!
- **For Mathematicians:** We build **Exact Carry-Presburger Residue-Transition Elimination (CPRTE)**, a standalone pure-Init Lean 4 reflection compiler that lifts finite pure-data residue-carry transition certificates (`Cert`, zero `Prop` fields) to universally quantified ($\forall \vec{x} \in \mathbb{N}^d$) and alternating ($\forall \vec{x} \in \mathbb{N}^d,\; \exists \vec{y} \in \mathbb{N}^e$) theorems mixing six atomic families under full Boolean connectives: linear inequalities/equalities, arbitrary-modulus congruences ($m \ge 1$), 4-way pointwise bitwise relations (`&`, `|`, `^`, disjointness, carry-word masks), exact binary carry-count bounds $\kappa(a, b, \gamma) \le K$ (proved equal to Kummer's popcount defect `carryCount_eq_popcount_defect`), and modular linear combinations of binary popcounts.

Under LSB-first bit-peeling, every atom admits an exact symbolic carry-residue derivative (`stepFormula`), and existential projection distributes across finite witness bit-sets inside the AST. Strong induction on $\sum x _ k$ yields three general soundness meta-theorems (`cert_sound`, `synthesizeY_sound`, `cert_sound_univ`), including an **executable 2-pass constructive Skolem transducer** `synthesizeY`. The compiler discharges 20 arithmetic-bitwise theorem statements (some of them finite sharpness checks) via kernel `decide` (zero `native_decide`), notably:
1. **Bounded-Carry Ternary Completion Phase Transition (`ternary_zero_carry_r0`, `ternary_carry1_r2`, `ternary_carry2_r0`):** Asymptotic slope $\alpha(K, r)$ for $\kappa(x, y) \le K \land (x+y) \equiv r \pmod 3$ exhibits a double parity-inversion phase transition: at $K=0$ slopes are $(5/3, 5/3, 2)$; at $K=1$, $r=2$ collapses $4\times$ to $(1, 1, 1/2)$; at $K=2$, $r=0,1$ drop to $1/4$ while $r=2$ stays blocked at $1/2$ — with matching extremal lower bounds proved in the library (`Challenge.lean` states the upper-bound witnesses `ternary_*`).
2. **Mersenne-Collatz Affine Zero-Carry Rigidity (`mersenne_collatz_rigidity_15`):** For $a \in \{3, 7, 15\}$ and all $x \in \mathbb{N}$, $x \mathbin{\&} (ax+1) = 0 \iff (ax+1) \mathbin{\&} (ax) = 0$ (sharp: fails at non-Mersenne $a = 5$).
- **Prior art & stack:** Presburger (1929) & Büchi–Bruyère arithmetic; Lean omega (linear only). Contribution claimed here (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search was recorded): a kernel-verified Carry-Presburger reflection compiler with an executable 2-pass Skolem transducer, and the bounded-carry ternary phase transition.

### 41. [`integer-mps-mpo-tensor-verifier/`](integer-mps-mpo-tensor-verifier/) — **93 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Quantum-inspired tensor networks (Matrix Product States and Operators) compress vectors of length $2^w$ and matrices of size $2^w \times 2^w$ into tiny fixed-size integer building blocks. We built an exact-integer tensor-network proof compiler in Lean 4 that compresses huge binary sums — like correlating the Rudin–Shapiro sequence with binary addition carries or taking the 4th spectral trace of the binary carry-parity matrix — down to minimal $2 \times 2$ integer certificates checked in milliseconds. Without using a single floating-point or irrational number, it proves exact infinite-width formulas for every bit width $w$: the carry-Walsh trace equals $2^w$ times a Fibonacci number, the carry and borrow operators are strictly orthogonal, and every odd power of the carry-parity matrix has trace zero!
- **For Mathematicians:** We construct **`SiliconTensorEngine`**, a two-stage exact integer Ho-Kalman / gauge-intertwiner proof compiler for binary Matrix Product States (MPS) and Matrix Product Operators (MPO) of arbitrary bond dimension $D$ over $\mathbb{Z}$. By LSB-first splitting and the Kronecker mixed-product law, exponentially large $2^w$-dimensional inner products, traces, Hilbert–Schmidt products, and expectation values reduce uniformly to finite $K \times K$ integer transfer systems $(u, T, v)$. Guaranteed denominator-free by Gauss's Lemma, our two-stage compiler (`sound_rightReductionCert`, `sound_orthogonalityCert`, `sound_recurrenceCert`) verifies right-invariant Krylov projections $TP = P M _ 1$ and left-invariant quotients $Q M _ 1 = M _ 2 Q$ purely in `Int`/`Bool` via kernel `decide` (zero floats, zero `native_decide`), alongside pointwise MPS/MPO gauge intertwiners proving operator identities for all $w, x, y \in \mathbb{N}$.

This engine discharges six infinite theorem families:
1. **Parametric Rudin–Shapiro $q$-Carry Collapse (`thm_rs_carry_parametric_closed`):** For all $q \in \mathbb{Z}$ and $w \in \mathbb{N}$, $(q+3) S _ q(w) = (q+2) 2^{w+1} + (-(q+1))^{w+1}$ (specializing to $2^w$ at $q=-1$, constant $1$ at $q=-2$, Jordan-block $(1-w)2^w$ at $q=-3$, and Jacobsthal at $q=0$).
2. **Carry-Parity 4th Moment & All-Odd-Trace Vanishing (`thm_carry_all_odd_traces_zero`):** $\mathrm{Tr}(C _ {w+2}^4) = 4 \mathrm{Tr}(C _ {w+1}^4) + 64 \mathrm{Tr}(C _ w^4)$ (irrational eigenvalues $2 \pm 2\sqrt{17}$ certified purely over $\mathbb{Z}$), and $\mathrm{Tr}(C _ w^{2m+1}) = 0$ for all $w \ge 1, m \in \mathbb{N}$.
3. **Carry–Walsh Fibonacci Law vs. Borrow Orthogonality (`thm_carry_hadamard_fibonacci`, `thm_carry_borrow_orthogonal`):** $\mathrm{Tr}(C _ w H _ w) = 2^w F _ {w+2}$, whereas $\mathrm{Tr}(C _ w B _ w) = 0$ and $\mathrm{Tr}(B _ w(-1) H _ w) = 0$ for all $w \ge 1$.
4. **Pointwise Carry/Borrow Complementarity & MPO Unitarity (`thm_carry_plus_borrow_tm`):** $((C _ w + B _ w) \cdot \mathrm{TM} _ w)(x) = 2$ for all $w \ge 1, x \in \mathbb{N}$, plus two-stage $4 \to 2 \to 1$ unitarity of Gray-code and Modular-Increment MPOs.
- **Prior art & stack:** Berstel–Reutenauer (1988) weighted finite automata; Schollwöck (2011) on MPS/MPO tensor networks. Contribution claimed (no prior-art search is recorded; not independently reviewed): a denominator-free two-stage exact-integer Ho-Kalman & gauge-intertwiner Lean 4 compiler, and infinite-width binary identities that the authors did not locate elsewhere (parametric RS q-carry, Carry-Walsh Fibonacci, all-odd-trace).

### 46. [`binary-gray-superposition-transfer/`](binary-gray-superposition-transfer/) — **93 / 100**
- **Plain English:** The package proves facts about one explicit family of graphs (each vertex is a `k`-bit number; two numbers are joined if they differ by 1, or are consecutive in the Gray code) for every size `k` at once, plus a few concrete small cases.
- **For Mathematicians:** `GB_k` is the graph on the vertices `{0, ..., 2^k - 1}` whose edges are the binary path edges `{i, i+1}` together with the Gray-code path edges `{gray i, gray (i+1)}`, where `gray i = i XOR (i >> 1)`. `BitTransfer3` is a transfer-tensor engine for 3-terminal graph families glued along the most significant bit; the perfect-matching count, independence number and chromatic count of `GB_k` are tied to small transfer vectors for all `k`.
- **Prior art & stack:** Formalizes a pure-Init Lean 4 proof development with zero Mathlib dependencies; `comparator.json` enables `nanoda`. Not independently reviewed; no prior-art search is recorded. Pure `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1` `Init` (zero Mathlib), `comparator` + `nanoda` verified.

E. Knuth, "Overlapping Pfaffians", *Electron. J. Combin.* 3(2) (1996), #R5) are classical in paper linear algebra. ### 48. [`binary-morphism-abelian-balance/`](binary-morphism-abelian-balance/) — **92 / 100**
- **Plain English:** An infinite binary word generated by a substitution rule is $C$-balanced if any two equal-length substrings differ in their count of 1s by at most $C$. Using exact integer arithmetic in a quadratic ring (zero floating point or reals), we build a modular-stratified balance verifier that proves balance upper bounds for infinite families of substitutions—including a bound whose formula changes at $k = 8$ and the rejection of one specific unstratified certificate for `mu1`.
- **For Mathematicians:** We build `MS-QR-PAIE`: a zero-real `Int × Int` quadratic-ring Brahmagupta compiler paired with a verified $O(\log n)$ Dumont-Thomas prefix oracle (`dtTakeOnes_spec`) and a pure-data modular-stratified certificate checker (`StratCert`, `stratCert_sound_balance`). By stratifying prefix discrepancies modulo $M$, a finite kernel-checked certificate discharges uniform abelian balance $| |w[i..i+n)| _ 1 - |w[j..j+n)| _ 1 | \le C$ across **all** positions $i, j$ and **all** lengths $n \in \mathbb{N}$. This engine proves three groups of statements, all upper bounds: (1) for the symbolic non-unimodular family $\sigma _ k$ ($\det = k$, $k \ge 1$), a uniform balance **upper bound** `phaseBound k` (`family_sigmaK_balance`; the piecewise-linear bound switches from `2k-3` to `2k-4` at $k = 8$, which the authors attribute to a quadratic discriminant `2k^2 - 21k + 48` that is not itself a Lean statement) paired with explicit infinite geometric prefix families whose discrepancies equal the stated quadratic-ring values (`family_sigmaK_prefix_sharp`); (2) **same-matrix balance bounds** (`tauA_balance_infinite` with bound $2$ and `tauB_balance_infinite` with bound $4$, for morphisms with identical incidence matrices; no doubling of the true balance is proved); and (3) **one unstratified-vs-stratified comparison** (`mu1_unstrat_impossible`: a stated inequality and the rejection of one specific unstratified certificate `mu1CertM1_failC2`; against `mu1_strat_balance_infinite` and `mu2_strat_balance_infinite`). There is no `mu2_unstrat_impossible` theorem in `Challenge.lean`.
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical combinatorics on words & balance lineage:** Dumont & Thomas (*Systèmes de numération et fonctions fractales relatifs aux substitutions*, TCS 1989), Adamczewski (*Balances for fixed points of primitive substitutions*, TCS 2003), Berthé & Tijdeman (*Balance properties of multi-dimensional words*, TCS 2002), Rauzy fractals, and Richomme, Saari & Zamboni (*Balance and Abelian complexity of the Tribonacci word*, Adv. Appl. Math. 2010). **Prior-art status:** not independently reviewed; no literature search was recorded. The authors believe the following are new but this is unchecked: the zero-real `Int × Int` Brahmagupta compiler with a zero-`Prop` `StratCert` checker, the `mu1` unstratified-certificate rejection, the symbolic non-unimodular $\sigma _ k$ bound `phaseBound k` with geometric prefix families, and the `tauA`/`tauB` bounds for same-matrix morphisms.

- **Prior art & stack:** Unifies Guo & Higham (2006) coupled $p$-th roots, Shampoo/SOAP/Muon, and `muon-vertex-polar/` / `soap-vertex-fourth-root/` into one all-$p$ statement with a rounding-model comparison of degree 5 and degree 9. ### 58. [`carry-prefix-threshold-monoid/`](carry-prefix-threshold-monoid/) — **91 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Adding many numbers at once, or adding and subtracting, makes carries bigger than one bit, and the carry maps seem to need ever more state as words get longer. We prove they never do: once a block of digits is wide enough, its whole effect on the carry is one of only $N ^ 2 - N + 1$ possible behaviours, whatever the word length and whatever shape of prefix tree combines the blocks.
- **For Mathematicians:** Multi-operand, signed-borrow, mixed-radix and modulo-$m$ carry transitions are Euclidean floor-division maps $c \mapsto \lfloor (c + S) / M \rfloor$. Their composition lives in the unbounded semidirect product $(\mathbb{N} _ {> 0}, \times) \ltimes (\mathbb{Z}, +)$, whose shift $S$ grows linearly with the word width. We prove that on every carry window $[L, U]$ and every block with $M \ge U - L$ the map **collapses faithfully** into the finite semigroup $\mathcal{T} _ {[L, U]}$ of threshold steps $c \mapsto q + [c \ge t]$ and constants, and that `collapse` is an exact semigroup homomorphism on window-preserving blocks. The semigroup is aperiodic ($e ^ 3 = e ^ 2$) and has exactly $N ^ 2 - N + 1$ elements for $N = U - L + 1$ carry states (the cardinality is proved in the library; the `Challenge.lean` statement `enumBounded_length` is the length of the hand-built enumeration list) ($N = 2$ recovers the classical generate/propagate monoid with $3$ elements, $N = 3$ gives $7$). Everything lifts to **all** prefix-tree topologies (ripple, Sklansky, Kogge-Stone, Brent-Kung, arbitrary unbalanced trees) and all widths through one generic associative `PrefixTree` chassis: two trees with the same fringe evaluate equally in any semigroup. Below the critical modulus the collapse is provably impossible. Global carry absorption reduces to a local adjacent condition (a two-state subshift of finite type): a tree absorbs the carry iff its fringe is not a resonant chain, giving exact counts for three-operand sums ($2 \cdot 4 ^ w$ resonant weighted triples out of $8 ^ w$), and modulo an odd $m > 1$ no binary block ever absorbs.
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical carry-lookahead and parallel prefix:** the two-input generate/propagate monoid and its flip-flop semigroup $U _ 2$ (Brent-Kung 1982, Ladner-Fischer 1980, Kogge-Stone 1973) and modulo $2 ^ n \pm 1$ adders (Zimmermann 1999) are credited as the $N = 2$ background. The $U _ 2$ flip-flop monoid and the CFL83 reference are credited as the closest classical antecedents. **Crandall primes:** the feedback dichotomies concern end-around carry for moduli of the form $2 ^ n \pm \delta$ in the sense of Crandall's special-form moduli. **Contributions claimed here (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search was recorded):** the generalization of the $U _ 2$ / CFL83-style monoid to unbounded multi-input carries with an exact collapse-width threshold, resonance-lock subshift counts and Crandall/Fermat dichotomies.

- **Plain English:** Reversible logic-gate cellular automata (built from Fredkin, Toffoli, and CNOT gates tiled in brickwork layers) evolve arbitrarily long rings of bits without losing information. We build a verified finite-window flux-certificate compiler that turns a tiny local check into exact conservation laws on infinite bit-lines and rings of every width, classifying all 256 Fredkin-CNOT gate masks and proving multi-charge conservation laws beyond simple Hamming weight.
- **For Mathematicians:** A local discrete continuity equation $q(\text{next}(u)) - q(u _ {\mathrm{out}}) = J(u _ {\mathrm{right}}) - J(u _ {\mathrm{left}})$ on an $M$-bit window is packaged as a zero-`Prop` pure-data record `FluxCert p r s M shiftOut`. Checking `c.verify = true` by kernel reduction over all $2 ^ M$ windows compiles generically via `FluxCert.open_segment_continuity` and `FluxCert.ring_conserved_iter` into exact Gauss-Noether charge-flux continuity on every open segment $[a, a + n)$ of every infinite bit-line $\mathbb{Z} \to \mathbb{B}$ and exact all-iteration conservation on every periodic ring $\mathrm{BitVec}\; (p \cdot n)$ ($n \ge 1$), closed under $\mathbb{Z}$-linear combinations (`linComb_verify`). We instantiate the engine across two reversible brickwork topologies (`Margolus4` and `SubCNOT`): a complete **256-gate 4-way `iff` classification** (`fredkin_cnot_iff_1_2` and `fredkin_cnot_classification_*`) proving that a 3-variable mask $h$ is ideal (the Boolean condition $h(a,c,d) \Rightarrow a \wedge (c \vee d)$, `IdealMask`) if and only if the finite `FluxCert` `certFredCNOT h` (fixed charge $q _ {\mathrm{FT}}$ and flux $J _ {\mathrm{FT}}$) passes (the 3-test-vector width-8 completeness is in `Theorems.lean`, not in `Challenge.lean`), exact all-width conservation and reversibility of the **Fredkin-Toffoli** lattice, a 3D **Double-Fredkin** charge algebra (Hamming weight, dimer, rail, and asymmetric charges), a cubic-flux Dual-Fredkin law, and non-Rule-54 **chiral SubCNOT** domain-wall conservation.

### 60. [`walnut-prophecy-skolem-lifter/`](walnut-prophecy-skolem-lifter/) — **91 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Take any whole number, add it to its reflected Gray code, and check whether the number of 1-bits in the sum is odd or even. Using a self-contained Lean 4 checker with backward-prophecy certificates that are far smaller than the forward ones (about 80x to 197x in state count, by the authors' own count), this package proves that this infinite binary sequence has consecutive doubled blocks only of lengths 1 through 7, consecutive tripled blocks only of length 1, and never five identical blocks in a row anywhere up to infinity.
- **For Mathematicians:** A pure-Init Lean 4 certificate verifier (`WalnutLifter`) for first-order base-2 automatic predicates combining four pure-data certificate lifters (`HomCert`, bidirectional `ExistsCert` with rank-certified trailing-zero saturation `ZeroReachCert`, `UniversalCert`, and the Backward-Prophecy / Bimachine Skolem lifter `ProphecyCert`, with certificates of 223 and 105 states against 17,895 and 20,719 for forward subset determinization, about 80x and 197x; this comparison is from the authors' script, not a Lean statement). Applied to the non-classical 2-automatic Gray-Sum Parity word $w _ {\oplus}(n) = \mathrm{popcount}(n + (n \oplus \lfloor n / 2 \rfloor)) \bmod 2$, it proves the exact finite square-period spectrum $\{1, \dots, 7\}$, the exact cube-period spectrum $\{1\}$, and fifth-power-freeness over all of $\mathbb{N}$.
- **Prior art & stack:** First-order decision procedures for automatic sequences originate with Buchi (1960), Bruyere-Hansel-Michaux-Villemaire (1994), and Mousavi's Walnut (2016; Shallit 2022); square-avoiding binary words were studied by Fraenkel-Simpson (1995) and Rampersad-Shallit-Wang (2005). ### 61. [`bsda-mixed-bitwise-perm-polys/`](bsda-mixed-bitwise-perm-polys/) — **91 / 100**
- **Plain English:** In cryptography and hardware hashing, you want a fast formula mixing addition, multiplication, shifts, XOR, AND, and OR that is guaranteed to be a bijection (invertible with no collisions) on **every** word size $2^w$ — 8-bit, 32-bit, 64-bit, or a million-bit words. For ordinary polynomials, checking mod $4$ is enough (Rivest 2001), but adding a single bitwise mask can make a formula invertible up to 64 bits and suddenly collide at 65 bits! We build a verified compiler in Lean 4 that translates mixed bitwise-arithmetic expressions into a finite automaton, computes the exact finite bit-width horizon $W^*$ needed to test it, and proves a 3-way equivalence: invertible at every width $\iff$ invertible at width $W^*$ $\iff$ accepted by our fast kernel checker.
- **For Mathematicians:** Classical permutation-polynomial criteria modulo $2^w$ (Rivest 2001) collapse to width $w = 2$ (mod $4$), but introducing bitwise masks (`&`, `|`), shifts (`<< k`), carries (`+`, `-`), and XOR (`^`) pushes the first collision to arbitrarily high widths $w = k+1$. We introduce the **Bit-Slice Derivative Automaton (BSDA)** engine (`MixedPermEngine`) directly on native `BitVec w`:

1. **Universal Bit-Slice Lifting & 11-Rule Differential Calculus (`bijective_iff_forall_bitDeriv`):** A truncation-coherent family $f _ w$ is bijective on $\mathrm{BitVec}\; w$ iff its bit-$i$ flip-derivative $\partial _ i f(x) = \mathrm{msb}(f _ {i+1}(\mathrm{cons}\; 1\; x)) \oplus \mathrm{msb}(f _ {i+1}(\mathrm{cons}\; 0\; x))$ equals `true` for all $i < w$ and all $x : \mathrm{BitVec}\; i$. Across all 10 silicon operators plus composition, $\partial _ i$ obeys exact Boolean laws where nonlinear multiplication collapses its bit-$(i+1)$ derivative to a 1-bit dependence on $x _ 0$.
2. **Pure-Data BSDA Compiler & Computable Finite-Slice Horizon (`bsda_bijective_iff`, `bsda_finite_slice_iff`):** `compileBSDA` compiles mixed bitwise-arithmetic expressions into a pure-data finite-state automaton `DerivAuto` (zero `Prop` fields) and computes a BFS reachability-stabilization horizon $W^*(D)$, yielding an **exact three-way `iff`** between $(\forall w \ge 2,\; \mathrm{Bijective}(\mathrm{eval} _ w(e)))$, kernel `decide` reflection `D.checkBijective = true`, and finite-slice bijectivity at width $W^*(D)$.
3. **Generalized Masked Mixed-Connective Rivest & Carry-Coupled `iff` Theorems (`maskedPoly_bijective_iff`, `rivest_classical_mod4_iff`, `carryPair_bijective_iff`, `addXorCancel_bijective_iff`, `klimovShamir_general_iff`):** Establishes exact bit-by-bit necessary and sufficient conditions for arbitrary masked, shifted, mixed-connective polynomials (recovering Rivest 2001 at $W^* = 2$), proves $F _ {c,d}(x) = x + (x \mathbin{\&} (x+c)) + (x \mathbin{\&} (x+d))$ is bijective mod $2^w$ iff $c \equiv d \pmod{2^w}$, and proves general MBA cancellation and Klimov–Shamir criteria for arbitrary coherent T-functions.
- **Prior art & stack:** Rivest (2001) permutation polynomials mod 2^w; Anashin (1994, 2002) & Klimov–Shamir (2002–2004) T-functions; Zhou–Main–Gu–Johnson (2007) & Eyrolles (2017) MBA permutations; contribution (prior-art status not established; no search was recorded): the pure-data BSDA compiler 3-way iff and the masked mixed-connective Rivest theorem.

### 62. [`automatic-word-avoidance-compiler/`](automatic-word-avoidance-compiler/) — **91 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Write down an infinite sequence of $0$s and $1$s by taking each whole number $n$, multiplying by $3$ or $5$, XORing or ANDing with $n$, and checking whether the result has an even or odd number of $1$-bits. Can the same block of digits repeat $4$, $5$, or $6$ times in a row anywhere in this infinite sequence — even a billion digits out? We built a self-verifying Lean 4 compiler that compiles these binary arithmetic formulas directly into finite automata, verifies a compact overlap-graph and parity-synchronization certificate inside the kernel, and proves repetition limits, each with a finite sharpness witness, for five different carry-bitwise and Gray-code sequences — including one (`carryXor 5`) that never has four identical digits in a row, yet contains a 5-fold repetition of a 3-digit block at position $153$, and never a 6-fold repetition anywhere.
- **For Mathematicians:** We introduce a four-layer pure-data certificate compiler (`SiliconAvoidance`) in standalone Lean 4 `Init` that bridges raw `Nat` bitwise/carry arithmetic (`*`, `^^^`, `&&&`, `/ 2`, `popcount`) directly to universally quantified unbounded pattern-avoidance theorems ($\forall n, p \in \mathbb{N},\; p \ge 1$) inside the Lean kernel (zero `native_decide`, zero external Walnut/Büchi solvers):

1. **Layer 0 — Carry-Offset Vector Compiler:** Finite state-vector tables checked by `decide` compile $\mathrm{carryXor}(K, n) = \mathrm{popcount}((Kn) \oplus n) \bmod 2$, $\mathrm{carryAnd}(K, n) = \mathrm{popcount}((Kn) \mathbin{\&} n) \bmod 2$, and $\mathrm{grayMod4}(n) = \mathrm{popcount}(n \oplus \lfloor n/2 \rfloor) \bmod 4$ directly to minimal MSB-first DFAOs.
2. **Layer 1 — LSB-Indexed `BTree` Overlap-Graph Engine (`windowCert_sound`):** Certifies window invariants (forbidden factors, abelian/Hamming-weight balance bounds, and finite base-period exclusions) in $O(\log N)$ kernel lookup time.
3. **Layer 2 — Bitmask Parity-Synchronization & Even-Halving $k$-Power Compiler (`powerAvoidCert_sound`):** Combines a parity-tagged synchronization graph `SyncCert`, an even-index decoder `invEven`, and finite base-period certificates to descend every period $p \ge p _ {\mathrm{sync}}$ to $p/2$ by strong induction.
4. **Layer 3 — Parity-Conditioned Both-Bit Halving Overlap Compiler (`overlapAvoidCert_sound`):** Handles words where unconditional single-bit halving fails (such as `grayMod4`), proving full overlap-freeness.

The compiler discharges unbounded avoidance theorems, each with a finite sharpness witness, for five distinct pure-silicon sequences: `carryXor 3` is 4-power-free (sharp: has cube `000`), `carryAnd 3` is 5-power-free (sharp: has 4th power `1111`), `carryAnd 5` is 4-power-free (sharp: has $(7/2)$-power), `carryXor 5` is 6-power-free (sharp: avoids 4-runs at $p=1$ yet attains a 5th power `(010)^5` at $p=3$!), and `grayMod4` is overlap-free (sharp: has square `1212`), plus abelian window balance bounds.
- **Prior art & stack:** Thue (1906), Allouche–Shallit, Mousavi (2016) / Shallit (2022) Walnut; contribution (prior-art status not established): a kernel-checked Lean 4 carry-offset DFAO + synchronized overlap-graph compiler and avoidance theorems (with finite sharpness witnesses) for 5 carry-bitwise/Gray words.

- **Plain English:** Proves closed-form worst-case one-step contraction constants (the $\alpha$ assumed in Error Feedback `EF21` analyses; the Lean statements are one-step compressor inequalities, not EF21 convergence) for compressing gradients: adding zero to 1-bit sign compression changes the constant from $1/d$ to $\Theta(1/\ln d)$ (about `375×` the 1-bit constant at $d = 1024$), leaving zero out of symmetric 2-bit collapses back to $O(1/d)$, and simplified models of the 4-bit formats `MXFP4` and `NVFP4` show multi-regime phase transitions across every block size $B \ge 1$ (the E8M0 scale and saturation are not modelled, ties round half up, and the NVFP4 scale is a continuous real).
- **For Mathematicians:** Universal exact one-step contraction constant $\alpha _ {\mathrm{tern}}(d) = (\sum _ {i=0}^{d-1} (\sqrt{i+1} - \sqrt{i})^2)^{-1}$ over $\mathbb{R}^d$ (proved via Abel summation by parts, tightly attained at $w _ i = \sqrt{i+1} - \sqrt{i}$) with exact harmonic sandwich $4 / (4 + H _ {d-1}) \le \alpha _ {\mathrm{tern}}(d) \le 4 / (3 + H _ d)$; exact multi-regime contraction constants for simplified models of OCP E2M1 MXFP4 ($\min(384/(B+399), 192/(B+195), 144/(B+143))$, 3 regimes, exact $48/53$ at $B = 16$ and $144/175$ at $B = 32$) and NVFP4 ($\min((24B+12)/(25B+11), 576/(B+575))$, 2 regimes, in the same simplified model, exact $132/137$ at $B = 16$ and $576/607$ at $B = 32$) for all $B \ge 1$; symmetric 2-bit zero-omission collapse $\alpha \le 9/(d+8)$; and exact $m$-bit mantissa stochastic rounding relative variance law $1/(4 \cdot 2^m (2^m + 1))$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Builds on Richtárik et al. (NeurIPS 2021) EF21, TernGrad (Wen et al. 2017), Horváth et al. (2019) Natural Compression, and OCP MXFP4 / Blackwell NVFP4. No prior record of the exact $\Theta(1/\ln d)$ ternary contraction constant or of these MXFP4/NVFP4 phase diagrams was found by the authors; the search was not recorded. Lean `v4.34.1` / Mathlib (`propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`).

### 69. [`carry-defect-weight-spectra/`](carry-defect-weight-spectra/) — **90 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Multiply a number by a constant two ways, once with ordinary carries and once with carry-less (XOR) arithmetic, and count how many bits differ. We determine exactly which bit-difference counts can occur, for every input, for whole infinite families of multipliers.
- **For Mathematicians:** For a multiplier $c$ and input $x$ define the carry defect $\Delta(c, x) = (c x) \oplus \mathrm{clmul}(c, x)$ between integer multiplication and carry-less multiplication in $\mathbb{F} _ 2 [x]$, its weight $W _ c (x) = \mathrm{popcount}(\Delta(c, x))$ and the spectrum $\mathcal{W}(c) = \{ W _ c (x) : x \in \mathbb{N} \}$. We prove: $\mathcal{W}(c) = \mathbb{N}$ for every $c \ge 5$ with $c \equiv 1 \pmod 4$; $\mathcal{W}(2 ^ m - 1) = \mathbb{N} \setminus \{1\}$ for every $m \ge 2$, together with an exact zero-defect criterion for $2 ^ m - 1$; and the sporadic spectra $\mathcal{W}(23) = \mathbb{N} \setminus \{1\}$, $\mathcal{W}(175) = \{0\} \cup [3, \infty)$ (the smallest multiplier with minimum nonzero weight $3$) and $\mathcal{W}(211) = \mathbb{N}$, the last via a weight-one defect at the single input $x = 17125$ that no short input reveals. The engine is a residual Moore transducer on pending integer and carry-less tails $(u, v)$ with $0 \le v \le u < c$, so the infinite family of inputs is governed by finitely many states. A pure-data binary trie of Bellman inequalities is checked by kernel evaluation and `PotentialCert.sound` compiles it into the universal bound $\forall x,\ \Delta(c, x) \ne 0 \implies \mu \le W _ c (x)$. Shift separation $W _ c (x + 2 ^ K y) = W _ c (x) + W _ c (y)$ makes $\mathcal{W}(c)$ an additive submonoid, so a certificate plus $\mu$ witnesses yields the exact spectrum.
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical background:** zero-carry addition ($x + y = x \oplus y \iff x \mathbin{\&} y = 0$), Fibbinary numbers (OEIS A003714, the $c = 3$ zero-defect baseline), carry Markov chains for integer addition (Holte 1997, Diaconis-Fulman 2009), and finite-state automatic sequences (Allouche-Shallit). **Contributions claimed here (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search was recorded):** only the $c = 3$ Fibbinary zero-defect baseline is treated as classical; the weight-spectrum submonoid classification, the residual Moore transducer with pure-data Bellman trie compiler, $\mathcal{W}(2 ^ m - 1) = \mathbb{N} \setminus \{1\}$, the minimal-$\mu$ multiplier $175$ and the $211$ stealth counterexample are the results of this package.

### 79. [`binary-lyness-periods/`](binary-lyness-periods/) — **89 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Repeating $(x, y) \to (y,\; x \oplus (y + 2^r))$ on **any** two whole numbers — no matter how huge — always snaps back to where you started after **exactly $6$ steps** (and this works **only** when the constant is $0$ or a power of $2$), while its bitwise-OR twin $(y,\; x \oplus (y \mathbin{|} (y + 2^r)))$ always snaps back in **exactly $12$ steps**.
- **For Mathematicians:** General XOR-Feistel carry-renormalization laws on unbounded $\mathbb{N} \times \mathbb{N}$: $f(2k) = 2k + 1, f(2k + 1) = 2h(k)$ forces $F _ f^6 = \mathrm{id}$ off odd-odd states via exact $F _ f^3$-invariance of $a \oplus b$, and $f(2k + 1) = 2g(k) + 1$ forces $F _ f^{12} = \mathrm{id}$ globally via conjugacy of $F _ f^4$ to $\mathbb{F} _ 2$-Fibonacci; implies $F _ c^6 = \mathrm{id} \iff c = 0 \lor \exists r,\, c = 2^r$ and $H _ {2^r}^{12} = \mathrm{id}$ for all $r$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Echoes classical rational Lyness recurrences (Lyness 1942) and contrasts with addition *outside* XOR (MathOverflow *"A Bitwise Xor Problem"*). No prior record or OEIS match for these laws or dichotomy was found by the authors; the search was not recorded. Lean `v4.34.1` / pure `Init` (`propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`).

### 80. [`carried-ca-coboundary-rigidity/`](carried-ca-coboundary-rigidity/) — **89 / 100**
- **Plain English:** A row of switches updated by a circuit with a rippling carry cannot keep a one-sided lid on a weighted count of lit switches unless the circuit does nothing, provided it loses no information and every state reaches every other. Yet a carried circuit built from the Rule 30 output and the Rule 120 carry (a hand-written transducer, not Wolfram's ordinary synchronous Rule 30 automaton) conserves an alternating count and the remainder modulo 3 exactly forever, and whole infinite families of circuits (Fredkin routers, bounded-walk involutions, a period-4 hybrid) obey exact laws for every size.
- **For Mathematicians:** Carried-hybrid 1D binary cellular automata are modelled as causal Mealy bit transducers. Theorem 1: a strongly connected causal bijective transducer with nowhere-zero weights and a one-sided bounded weighted defect is the identity (defect unbounded on both sides otherwise). Carried Rule 30 T30 = mkCHCA2 30 120 (a hand-written sequential transducer, not the synchronous Rule 30 automaton) conserves the alternating sum exactly under all zero-flushed iterates and semigroup compositions with T46 (together with the value mod 3); the De Morgan swap T120 conserves popcount; Fredkin routers F_d and bounded-walk involutions W_K give infinite parametric families; T54_105 has exact period 4 on all widths. All via finite coboundary and bisimulation certificates lifted by induction.
- **Prior art & stack:** Local-window CA conservation (Hattori-Takesue 1991, Boccara-Fuks 1998, Moreira 2003) is finite-radius; causal bijectivity of synchronous Mealy automata is classical (Grigorchuk, Sidki, Nekrashevych) and is used as a foundation. The weighted-defect rigidity law, the Rule 30 / Rule 120 conservation, the Fredkin and walk families and the certified period-4 lift are the contributions claimed here; no prior-art search was recorded, and the package has not been independently reviewed.

### 81. [`dualrail-tropical-carry-horizon/`](dualrail-tropical-carry-horizon/) — **89 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Redundant binary addition lets a digit be $-1$, $0$ or $+1$, so carries never travel far. We prove, for numbers of every length, that this adder computes the exact sum, is associative and commutative, and that each output digit depends only on a window of three input positions, while ordinary ripple carry provably has no such window.
- **For Mathematicians:** Dual-rail digits are pairs of bits $(p, n)$ with value $p - n$. For a finite transducer $T$ over streams, a finite **potential certificate** $\varphi$ yields the exact identity $\mathrm{prefixVal} _ w (\text{out}) + 2 ^ w \varphi(\text{state} _ w) = \mathrm{prefixSum} _ w (\text{target}) + \varphi(q)$ at every width $w$, and a finite **relational invariant** between two transducers yields equality of prefix values modulo $2 ^ w$ at every width. Applied to the two-stage radix-2 dual-rail adder this gives exact integer semantics (`avizAdd_exact_int`) and associativity modulo $2 ^ w$ on arbitrary streams (`avizAdd_assoc_mod`), even though the adder is neither raw-commutative nor raw-associative at the bit level. Carry-boundedness is certified by a finite pair-Lyapunov rank certificate and $2 \times 2$ tropical max-plus matrices whose product is rank one; every output digit depends only on a window of $2$ preceding input positions (`avizAdd_horizon2`), and for chains of adders the exact lag is attained by an explicit one-position perturbation (`exact_horizon_achieved`). Ripple carry has no horizon certificate for any $H$ (`no_horizon_cert_for_ripple`). A verified compiler flattens any dual-rail expression tree to a right chain preserving the value modulo $2 ^ w$ for all $w$ (`compileRight_equiv_all_widths`).
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical background:** Avizienis (1961) signed-digit carry-free addition, Chow-Robertson (1978) and Frougny (1992) on redundant binary automata, and Parhami's redundant number system surveys; max-plus/tropical matrix semigroups (Baccelli et al.). **Contributions claimed here (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search was recorded):** the reusable certificate lifters, the tropical rank-$1$ horizon calculus with exact `TailPulse` tightness, the horizon-halving equivalence compiler and the encoding-independent radix-$r$ horizon-$1$ obstruction.

### 83. [`carry-run-cyclotomic-transfer/`](carry-run-cyclotomic-transfer/) — **89 / 100**
- **Plain English:** When a computer adds two $w$-bit numbers, carry bits ripple from right to left. Take every pair of $w$-bit numbers whose longest unbroken carry chain is at most $L$, add them, and score each pair $+1$ or $-1$ according to whether the sum has an even or odd number of $1$-bits. Out of $4^w$ possible pairs, you would expect the total to fluctuate wildly; instead, cancellations are so rigid that the entire sum collapses to just $-1$, $0$, or $+1$, repeating with exact period $L+2$ for every $L$ and every bit width $w$! Conversely, if you cap true hardware ripple-carry depth at $L$ and weight by the parity of the inputs instead of the output, the sum collapses to $0$ on every width not divisible by $L+1$. Swapping either pairing immediately breaks the collapse into exponential growth.
- **For Mathematicians:** We introduce `CarryTransferEngine`, a pure-Init Lean 4 framework that lifts finite radix-$B$ carry transducers and finite algebraic operator certificates (`arithSum_eq_transferPow`, `intertwining_lift`, `scaled_periodic_lift`, `trinomial_gf_cauchy`) to universally quantified arithmetic identities over all widths $w \in \mathbb{N}$, bounds $L \ge 1$, even radices $B = 2m$, and integer weights $t \in \mathbb{Z}$. We prove two determinant-free $(L+1) \times (L+1)$ parametric matrix annihilators (`rippleOp_annihilator` and `runOp_annihilator`) and establish a sharp duality between hardware carry metrics and Thue–Morse parity weights:

1. **Carry-run output-parity cyclotomic collapse (`evenRadix_carryRun_outputParity_collapse`, `binary_carryRun_fullPopcount_collapse`):** Summing $(-1)^{\sigma _ {2m}(w, (x+y) \bmod (2m)^w)}$ over all $(x,y) \in [0, (2m)^w)^2$ with maximum carry-bit run length $\le L$ collapses via hyperplane intertwining with cyclic rotation on $\mathbb{Z}^{L+2}$ to exact period $L+2$, taking values $+m^w$ ($w \equiv 0$), $-m^w$ ($w \equiv L+1$), and $0$ otherwise $\pmod{L+2}$ (and in binary, including the MSB carry-out bit, taking exact period-$(L+2)$ values $1, -2, 0, \dots, 0, 1$).
2. **Ripple-depth input-parity monomial collapse (`evenRadix_rippleDepth_inputParity_collapse`, `binary_rippleDepth_plain_recurrence`):** Summing $(-1)^{\sigma _ {2m}(w, x) + \sigma _ {2m}(w, y)}$ over pairs with ripple-carry propagation depth $\le L$ vanishes identically whenever $(L+1) \nmid w$ and equals $((-1)^{L+1} 2^L m^{L+1})^{w/(L+1)}$ whenever $(L+1) \mid w$, while the unweighted binary count satisfies $R _ L(w+L+1) = 4 R _ L(w+L) - 2^L R _ L(w)$.
3. **Bivariate absorption law & zero-absorption dichotomy (`binary_carryRun_absorption_recurrence`, `binary_zeroAbsorption_dichotomy`):** Weighting each carry absorption ($c _ i = 1 \to c _ {i+1} = 0$) by $t \in \mathbb{Z}$ yields $A _ L(t, w+L+2) = (1-t) A _ L(t, w+L) + t A _ L(t, w)$ for all $t, L, w$. At $t = 0$, $A _ L(0, w) = 0$ for all $w \ge L$ when $L$ is odd (exact finite support) versus $A _ L(0, w) = (-1)^w$ for all $w \ge L$ when $L$ is even.
- **Prior art & stack:** Burks–Goldstine–von Neumann (1946), Knuth (TAOCP 4A), Pippenger (2002) for unweighted ripple-carry chains; Holte (1997) and Diaconis–Fulman (2009) for carry chains; the cyclotomic carry-run / monomial ripple-depth Thue–Morse duality and bivariate absorption dichotomy are claimed here (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search was recorded).

### 84. [`gray-carry-monodromy-freezing/`](gray-carry-monodromy-freezing/) — **89 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Gray codes flip a single bit between consecutive numbers by XORing a number with a shifted copy of itself. What happens if you mix a Gray code with ordinary binary addition modulo $2^w$? For a right-shift Gray code, bits flow in both directions at once (rightward from the shift, leftward from carries), yet compiling the equation into a small $k$-bit feedback register proves that for every word width past a sharp threshold $L+2k+1$, there are always **exactly $2^k$ fixed points** — while subtracting instead of adding can trap every single candidate and leave **zero** solutions. For a left-shift Gray code, a carry-parity conservation law freezes the entire cycle structure forever: for every $c \equiv 9 \pmod{32}$ the map splits all $2^w$ numbers into **exactly 5 cycles** for every width $w \ge 5$, and for every $c \equiv 19 \pmod{64}$ into **exactly 7 cycles** for every $w \ge 6$.
- **For Mathematicians:** Hybrid Gray-code + modular-increment maps collide bitwise linear shift-XOR operators with carry-propagating addition modulo $2^w$. Depending on shift direction, we build two dual finite-to-infinite pure-silicon lifting engines:

1. **Bidirectional Delay-Feedback Transducer Reduction (BDFTR, `bdftr_exact_count`, `grayRight_nat_stabilization`, `grayRight_sub_complete_annihilation`):** Right-shift equations $x \gg k = \Phi _ w(x)$ couple MSB-to-LSB shifting with an LSB-to-MSB Mealy carry transducer, placing them outside 2-adic T-function theory. Compiling the transducer into a $k$-bit Delay-Feedback Shift Register (DFSR) puts solutions $x < 2^w$ in exact bijection with $k$-bit seeds whose step-$w$ delay buffer flushes to $0^k$. This proves that $((x \oplus (x \gg k)) + c) \bmod 2^w = x \oplus m$ has **exactly $2^k$ solutions** for all $k \ge 1$, $c, m < 2^L$, and $w \ge L + 2k + 1$ (stabilizing in $\mathbb{N}$, with bit-exact sharpness at $w = 3k+1$ for $c = 2^{k+1}-1$), whereas flipping $+c$ to $-d$ creates a carry-trap sink $(1, 1^k)$ that annihilates all solutions ($0$ for $d = 2^{2k+1}-2^k$, $2^k - 1$ for $d = 1$).
2. **Carry-Parity Monodromy Freezing Engine (CPMF, `cpmf_full_partition_lift`, `lgray1_mod32_9_exact_five_cycles`, `lgray1_mod64_19_exact_seven_cycles`, `lgray_ge2_odd_single_cycle`):** For left-shift Gray maps $L _ {k,c,w}(x) = (((x \oplus (x \ll k)) \bmod 2^w) + c) \bmod 2^w$, we prove a universal pair-carry conservation law: $\mathrm{carry} _ {w+1}(u) \oplus \mathrm{carry} _ {w+1}(u \oplus 2^w) = c _ w \oplus \mathrm{carry} _ w(u \bmod 2^w)$. A kernel-decidable base-width certificate (`checkBaseCert`) lifts directly to exact infinite cycle decompositions for all $w \ge w _ 0$, proving frozen 5-cycle spectra for all $c \equiv 9 \pmod{32}$ ($w \ge 5$), frozen 7-cycle spectra for all $c \equiv 19 \pmod{64}$ ($w \ge 6$), and universal single-cycle ergodicity for all $k \ge 2$ and odd $c$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Klimov–Shamir (2002, 2004) & Anashin–Khrennikov (2009) for single-cycle T-functions (Thm 7); the BDFTR bidirectional DFSR engine, 3k+1 sharpness, carry-trap annihilation, and CPMF frozen 5/7-cycle infinite spectra are the package's own statements (no prior record found by the authors; search not recorded; not independently reviewed).

We prove the general **orbit-lifting theorem** (`orbit_lift_exact_period`): a base orbit of exact period $L$ whose monodromy permutation has exact period $d$ on a fiber point lifts to an exact orbit of period $d \cdot L$. Restricting to the dihedral subgroup $D _ 4$ of sheared translations yields a sharp parity criterion (`full_period_of_D4_parity`) and **$D _ 4$ cocycle decoupling**: for arbitrary causal perturbations $P, Q, R$, `family1 P Q R` achieves full period $4 ^ w$ at every width $w$ (`family1_full_period`). Conversely, we prove the **universal no-shear obstruction** (`no_shear_never_full_period`): if every fiber action is a pure translation (zero shear), then $T _ w ^ {2 ^ w} = \mathrm{id}$ and full period $4 ^ w$ is impossible at every width $w \ge 1$. ### 89. [`bit-sliced-affine-shear-groups/`](bit-sliced-affine-shear-groups/) — **89 / 100**

Even though binary carries couple every bit non-linearly, taking the XOR difference of two inputs that agree on lower bits cancels the carry cleanly, exposing a tiny $6$-element linear group $\mathrm{GL} _ 2(\mathbb{F} _ 2)$ at every bit slice. Using this finite-to-infinite lens, we prove exact formulas for circuit orders at **every** bit width $w \ge 2$, show that $2^w$ simple 3-gate circuits all live in distinct conjugacy classes, prove that repeated commutators collapse completely in $w+1$ steps, and demonstrate a sharp structural jump: a single swap of bits $0$ and $1$ immediately breaks out of the $2^a 3^b$ universe by introducing prime factor $5$.

The core invariant `IsBitAffine r M f` — base-block causality below bit $r$ plus differential bit-slice linearity $(f(x)) _ k \oplus (f(y)) _ k = M(k) \cdot (x _ k \oplus y _ k)$ with $M(k) \in \mathrm{GL} _ 2(\mathbb{F} _ 2) \cong S _ 3$ — turns the bit-slice Jacobian profile into a group homomorphism $\Phi _ {w, r} : \mathcal{A} _ w^{(r)} \to (\mathrm{GL} _ 2(\mathbb{F} _ 2))^{[r, w)}$ whose kernel admits one-line squaring and commutator lifts.

This single finite-to-infinite engine establishes:
1. **Master Order Bound & Derived-Series Collapse (`bsasce_master_order_bound`, `bsasce_derived_series_collapse`):** Any $f \in \mathcal{A} _ w^{(r)}$ with base-block period $D$ and $M _ f(k)^D = I _ 2$ obeys $f^{D \cdot 2^{w-r}} = \mathrm{id}$, and the $(w+1)$-th derived subgroup $(\mathcal{A} _ w^{(0)})^{(w+1)}$ collapses identically to $\mathrm{id}$ (contrast: lower central series exceeds class $w$ at $w = 4$).
2. **Exact $3 \cdot 2^w$ Saturation & Non-Conjugacy (`theorem1_circuitE_exact_order`, `theorem2_circuitP_exact_order`, `theorem2_circuitFm_pairwise_non_conjugate`):** The masked shear $E = A _ R(0) \circ A _ {L, \{0\}}$ saturates the universal $\mathcal{A} _ w^{(0)}$ ceiling at exact order $3 \cdot 2^w$ (never a commutator and not conjugate even in $\mathrm{Sym}$ to any uniform-Jacobian circuit in $\mathcal{U} _ w$), $P = S _ {\{0\}} \circ A _ R(0)$ has exact order $3 \cdot 2^{w-1}$, and the $2^w$ circuits $F _ m = S _ m \circ A _ R(0) \circ X _ L(0)$ are pairwise non-conjugate in $\mathcal{A} _ w^{(0)}$.
3. **Add-XOR Commutator Tower & Wreath Normal Form (`theorem3_addXorShear_exact_order`, `theorem3_fiber1_wreath_normal_form`):** Both $A _ R(0) \circ X _ R(0)$ and $[A _ R(0), X _ R(0)]$ have exact order $2^{w-1}$, and the fiber-$1$ group $\langle +1, \oplus 1 \rangle$ is in exact canonical bijection with $C _ {2^{w-1}} \wr C _ 2$ of cardinality $2^{2w-1}$.
4. **Block-2 Prime-5 Symmetry-Breaking (`theorem4_circuitH_prime5_obstruction`):** Adding a single intra-lane bit-swap $B _ {0, 1}$ introduces prime factor $5$ ($H^{3 \cdot 2^w}(0, 1) \ne (0, 1)$ for all $w \ge 2$), proving the strict 3-level hierarchy $\mathcal{U} _ w \subsetneq \mathcal{A} _ w^{(0)} \subsetneq \mathcal{A} _ w^{(2)} \subsetneq \mathrm{Sym}((\mathrm{BitVec}\; w)^2)$.
