### 90. [`feistel-aff2-monodromy-locks/`](feistel-aff2-monodromy-locks/) — **89 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Feistel networks are the workhorse structure behind modern block ciphers: split a register into left and right halves $(L, R)$ and update $(L, R) \to (R, L \oplus f(R))$. We prove that whenever $f$ is built from standard binary hardware operations (addition, subtraction, AND, OR, XOR, NOT, left-shifts), the period of **every** state at **every** bit width $w$ can only ever be a product of powers of $2$ and $3$ ($2^a 3^b$) — no prime factor $5, 7, 11, \dots$ can ever appear! Even more strikingly, tiny sign flips trigger sharp phase transitions: $R+1$ snaps every state back in at most $6$ steps regardless of bit width, while $R-1$ explodes to period $3 \cdot 2^{w-1}$; $R \mathbin{\&} (-R)$ produces an exact Pascal-triangle spectrum of periods $3^j$, while $R \mathbin{\&} \sim(R+1)$ collapses to period $6$ forever.
- **For Mathematicians:** For any balanced XOR-Feistel network $F _ w(L, R) = (R, L \oplus f _ w(R))$ on $(\mathrm{BitVec}\; w)^2$ whose round function family $\{f _ w\}$ is triangular (2-adic 1-Lipschitz), we formalize the **Bit-Slice $\mathrm{Aff}(\mathbb{F} _ 2^2)$-Monodromy Tower Engine** (`FeistelMonodromy`). Because top-bit evolution factors through the 24-element affine group $\mathrm{Aff}(\mathbb{F} _ 2^2) \cong S _ 4$ (whose cycle lengths lie in $\{1, 2, 3, 4\}$), every state at every bit-width $w$ has exact minimal period $2^a 3^b$ (`feistel_universal_2_3_period`). Finite cancellation identities inside $\mathrm{Aff}(\mathbb{F} _ 2^2)$ lift to four universal infinite-width theorems across eight concrete hardware circuits (`R+1`, `R-1`, `R|(R±1)`, `R^(R±1)`, `R&(-R)`, `R&~(R+1)`):

1. **Universal Trigger-Independent Period-12 Lock (`theorem_I_universal_period12_lock`):** For every 2-adic trigger stream $\tau$, Rule-$(1,0,0,1)$ locks globally to period $12$ (covering both $R \mathbin{|} (R+1)$ and $R \mathbin{|} (R-1)$ for all $w \ge 1$).
2. **Sharp `+1` Period-6 Lock vs `-1` Exponential Escape (`theorem_IIa_add_one_period6_lock`, `theorem_IIb_sub_one_exponential_escape`):** $R+1$ locks universally to period $6$, whereas replacing $+1$ by $-1$ shatters the lock so $(0, 0)$ has exact minimal period $3 \cdot 2^{w-1}$ for all $w \ge 1$.
3. **Exact Binomial Power-of-3 Spectrum & $\mathrm{ctz}(\tau)$ Phase Transition (`theorem_IIIa_lsb_binomial_spectrum`, `theorem_IIIb_ctz_period_lock`):** For $R \mathbin{\&} (-R)$, exactly $\binom{w}{j} 3^j$ states have exact minimal period $3^j$ (maximum $3^w$), whereas any trigger $\tau$ with first `true` bit at $m$ collapses universally to constant global period $2 \cdot 3^{m+1}$ (locking $R \mathbin{\&} \sim(R+1)$ to period $6$).
4. **Universal Isoperiodic $2^{w+1}$ Law (`theorem_IV_universal_isoperiodic`):** Rule-$(0,0,0,1)$ (including $R \oplus (R+1)$ and $R \oplus (R-1)$) satisfies universal exact period bounds across all $w \ge 1$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Luby–Rackoff (1988) Feistel networks; Klimov–Shamir (2002, 2004) & Anashin–Khrennikov (2009) T-functions; the Aff(F_2^2) monodromy tower, universal 2^a 3^b law, R+1 vs R-1 dichotomy, binomial 3^w spectrum, and ctz(tau) phase transition are claimed here (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search was recorded).

### 91. [`fenwick-trigger-chain-monoid/`](fenwick-trigger-chain-monoid/) — **89 / 100**
- **Plain English:** A Fenwick tree (binary indexed tree) navigates indices using two low-bit jumps: $U(x) = x + \mathrm{lowbit}(x)$ and $D(x) = x - \mathrm{lowbit}(x)$. We prove that any sequence of $L$ jumps compiles into a minimal bit-streaming machine of linear size (instead of exponential $3 ^ L$), that the jumps generate a completely free monoid on all natural numbers yet collapse to an exact $2 ^ w$-element nilpotent monoid on $w$-bit words, and that two's-complement negation swaps $U$ and $D$ identically.
- **For Mathematicians:** Let $U(x) = x + \mathrm{lowbit}(x)$ and $D(x) = x - \mathrm{lowbit}(x)$ be the Fenwick tree update and query operators (bridged to hardware `x &&& (-x)` via `lowbit_bitvec_eq`). Naive composition of $L$ LSB Mealy transducers over $\{U, D\} ^ *$ produces $3 ^ L$ states. We build a 4-rule **Fenwick Trigger-Chain Compiler** (`compileTC`) that compiles any word $w \in \{U, D\} ^ *$ into an exact LSB Mealy transducer (`evalWord_mod_eq_compile`) and prove **Myhill-Nerode minimality** (`compileTC_minimal`): no LSB transducer with fewer states can compute $w$ modulo $2 ^ n$ for all $n$. This machinery discharges four exact infinite laws: (1) the **Free-vs-Nilpotent Monoid Dichotomy** — $\{U, D\} ^ *$ acts faithfully as a *free monoid* on unbounded $\mathbb{N}$ (`fenwick_free_monoid`), yet modulo $2 ^ w$ collapses to a finite monoid of exact cardinality $2 ^ w$ (`fenwick_finite_monoid_card`); (2) **Universal Ancestor-Spine Collapse** (`evalWord_D_spine`, `evalWord_U_spine`), showing every word beginning with $D$ or $U$ collapses onto the pure $D$- or $U$-spine; (3) **Two's-Complement Word-Conjugation Duality** (`evalWord_twos_complement_duality`); and (4) **Exact Fenwick Depth Duality** (`fenwick_depth_duality`), relating update depth to popcount and trailing zeros.
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical Fenwick trees & automata:** Fenwick (*A new data structure for cumulative frequency tables*, Software: Practice and Experience, 1994) introduced $x \pm (x \mathbin{\&} -x)$ for prefix sums; Myhill-Nerode minimality for finite-state transducers is classical. **Contents (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search is recorded):** the 4-rule Fenwick Trigger-Chain Compiler with exact linear Myhill-Nerode minimality, the free-vs-$2 ^ w$-nilpotent monoid dichotomy, universal $D$/$U$ ancestor-spine collapse, and exact two's-complement word-conjugation and depth dualities.

### 92. [`two-way-bit-transducer-inverses/`](two-way-bit-transducer-inverses/) — **89 / 100**
- **Plain English:** A one-way bit-streaming circuit (like a ripple counter) returns to the identity after $2 ^ w$ steps on $w$-bit numbers. Yet pairing it with a backward sweep in a two-way bit-transducer can make it return to the identity after only 3 or 6 steps for EVERY bit-width. We build a finite tile-witness crossing-sequence verifier that certifies all-width inversion and these identities (the Challenge states identities of the form T^n = id, not least periods).
- **For Mathematicians:** We introduce a pure-data **Finite Tile-Witness Crossing-Sequence Certificate** (`FiniteTileCert Q`, `checkCert_sound_bv`) for compositions of forward (LSB-to-MSB) and backward (MSB-to-LSB) bit-transducers. A finite local compatibility check on single-bit tiles lifts via inductive crossing-sequence witnesses to exact identity laws `sweepsBV cert.sws x = x` on `BitVec w` for **every** width $w \in \mathbb{N}$. Instantiating the engine yields bidirectional periodicity identities: (1) `skipFlip2TwoWay` satisfies $T ^ 3 = \mathrm{id}$ on `BitVec w` for all $w$ (`skipFlip2_period3_bv`), while its unidirectional forward sweep satisfies $T ^ {2 ^ w} = \mathrm{id}$ (`skipFlip2_fwd_period_pow2`; least periods are not stated in the Challenge); (2) shifting by one state gives $T ^ 6 = \mathrm{id}$ (`skipFlip2_shifted_period6_bv`); (3) for every run-length threshold $m \ge 2$, both `runTriggerTwoWay m` and `runSkipFlip m` satisfy $T ^ 6 = \mathrm{id}$ across all widths (the name `Coxeter-6 phase transition` is the authors' label) (`runTrigger_period6_all_m_bv`, `runSkipFlip_period6_all_m_bv`) and explicit two-way left inverses (`runTriggerTwoWay_left_inv`); and (4) coupled parity-delay forward sweeps are exactly inverted by Gray-difference backward sweeps (`coupled_parityDelay_exact_inv`).
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical two-way automata & crossing sequences:** Shepherdson (1959) and Rabin & Scott (1959) introduced crossing-sequence arguments for two-way finite automata; dihedral / Coxeter-group involutions and Gray-code differentiation are classical. **Novelty:** the authors did not find the `FiniteTileCert` crossing-sequence compiler for `BitVec w`, the bidirectional period-3 vs. forward-$2 ^ w$ collapse, the parametric $m \ge 2$ phase transition, or the coupled parity-delay inversion in the sources above; no search was recorded and the claim is not independently reviewed.

### 93. [`valuation-tower-poincare-recurrences/`](valuation-tower-poincare-recurrences/) — **89 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Take $d$ binary registers of any bit width $w$ and repeatedly replace the oldest register with $\Phi(\text{other registers}) - x _ 0 \pmod{2^w}$, where $\Phi$ is a nonlinear bitwise rule (even one involving squaring and multiplying registers!). Because modular subtraction ripples carries across all $w$ bits, you would expect orbits to scramble or grow exponentially with $w$. Instead, we prove that a simple 2-adic symmetry locks **every** orbit in **every** bit width $w$ to an exact universal period $Q(d) = \mathrm{lcm}(2d, d+1)(2d-1)$ — and even more weirdly, $Q(d)$ is **not monotone** in dimension $d$: 4D has period $280$, while 5D drops to $270$; 6D has period $924$, while 7D drops to $728$!
- **For Mathematicians:** Consider the $d$-dimensional ($d = m + 2 \ge 2$) nonlinear carry-propagating modular subtraction recurrence on $(\mathrm{BitVec}\; w)^d$: $F _ \Phi(x _ 0, \dots, x _ {d-1}) = (x _ 1, \dots, x _ {d-1},\; \Phi(x _ 1, \dots, x _ {d-1}) - x _ 0) \pmod{2^w}$. We formalize **2-Adic Valuation-Tower Poincaré Reduction (VTPR)**: whenever $\Phi$ obeys only three finite-depth 2-adic axioms (`ValuationTowerSpec`) relative to an odd constant $c$ — odd-pinning ($\Phi(v) = c$), double-pinning at valuation $1$ ($\Phi(v) = 2c$), and level-$\ge 2$ evenness + sign-invariance ($\Phi(-v) = \Phi(v)$ when $4 \mid v$), with **no** restriction on nonlinearity at valuation $\ge 2$ — every primitive orbit collapses via a two-stage Poincaré reduction (`poincare_return`, `poincareR_linearization`) to the pure linear monodromy $M = -\mathrm{negShift}$ satisfying $M^d = (-1)^{d+1} \mathrm{id}$.

This forces a striking **non-monotone dimension-parity period law**: $Q(d) = \mathrm{lcm}(2d, d+1)(2d-1)$ ($36, 60, 280, 270, 924, 728, 2160, 1530, \dots$, dropping at every odd $d \ge 5$). Layer 2 (`ValuationHomogeneousSpec`, `vtpr_full_period`) lifts primitivity across all valuation strata $0 \le \nu _ 2(x) \le w$ by strong induction on bit-width $w$, proving exact conservation of the joint lowest-set-bit $\mathrm{lsb}(x _ 0 \mid \cdots \mid x _ {d-1})$ and global period $Q(d)$ for all $w \in \mathbb{N}$. Moreover, `vtpr_universal_exact_minimal_period` proves that for every $d \ge 2$, every $w \ge 4$, and every $\Phi$ satisfying `ValuationTowerSpec`, the explicit state $(1, 0, \dots, 0, 4)$ achieves **exact minimal period $Q(d)$** — instantiated for both twisted joint-LSB subtraction (`twistedLSB_exact_minimal_period`) and the non-transducer wild quadratic family $4 \sum v _ j^2$ (`wildQuad_exact_minimal_period`).
- **Prior art & stack:** Discrete integrable / Lyness recurrences (field/tropical setting); the 2-adic Valuation-Tower Poincare Reduction (VTPR), joint-LSB conservation, non-monotone Q(d) = lcm(2d, d+1)(2d-1) law, and non-transducer quadratic coverage are claimed here (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search was recorded).

### 94. [`weighted-carry-jet-rationality/`](weighted-carry-jet-rationality/) — **89 / 100**
- **Plain English:** When you add and subtract two random $w$-digit numbers in base $B$, are the total number of carries and the total number of borrows correlated? We build a general bilinear jet-lift and Krylov generating-function engine over exact integers and prove a sharp parity dichotomy across all bases $B \ge 2$: in every *odd* base carries and borrows have **exact zero covariance**, while in every *even* base their covariance is an **exact signed perfect square**!
- **For Mathematicians:** We build a reusable **Weighted Carry-Transducer Bilinear Jet-Lift** (`CorrMachine S A → 4 S × 4 S jetMat`, `jetMat_pow_sound`) coupled to a decidable $O(d N ^ 2)$ **Left/Right-Krylov Annihilator Rationality Engine** (`recurrence_of_leftKrylov`, `fps_rational_of_recurrence`, `closedForm_of_recurrence`) operating strictly in exact integer formal power series $\mathbb{Z}[[t]]$ (zero floats, zero reals), bridged directly to hardware radix-$B$ overflow and underflow predicates (`radixCarryState_eq_div`, `radixBorrowState_eq_lt`). Instantiating the engine discharges: (1) exact rational generating functions in $\mathbb{Z}[[t]]$ and all-4-state closed forms for binary carry-borrow and parity-twisted correlations (`binary_carry_borrow_gf`, `binary_parity_corr_gf`); (2) the exact **Binary Triangular-Square Covariance Law** (`binary_triangular_square_covariance`): $\mathrm{Cov}(C, B) = \mp (2 ^ {w - 1}(2 ^ w - 1)) ^ 2 / 4 ^ w$; and (3) the universal **Arbitrary-Radix $B \ge 2$ Even/Odd Dichotomy** — exact zero carry-borrow covariance in every odd base $B = 2 m + 1$ (`odd_radix_zero_covariance`), exact signed perfect-square covariance in every even base $B = 2 m$ (`even_radix_perfect_square_covariance`), and exact even-vanishing vs. odd-growth parity partition sums (`radix_parity_partition_formula`).
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical carry Markov chains & transfer matrices:** Holte (*Carries, Combinatorics, and an Amazing Matrix*, AMM 1997) and Diaconis & Fulman (*Carries, shuffling, and symmetric functions*, Adv. Appl. Math. 2009) study carry Markov chains for addition; transfer-matrix rationality in $\mathbb{Z}[[t]]$ is classical (Stanley, *Enumerative Combinatorics* Vol. 1). **Novelty:** the authors did not find the mechanized bilinear $4 S \times 4 S$ jet-lift with a decidable Krylov rationality engine in $\mathbb{Z}[[t]]$, the exact binary triangular-square covariance law, or the radix-$B$ even/odd zero-vs-perfect-square carry-borrow covariance and parity-partition dichotomies in the sources above; no search was recorded and the claim is not independently reviewed.

### 95. [`self-delimiting-prefix-transducers/`](self-delimiting-prefix-transducers/) — **89 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Self-delimiting binary codes (like Fibonacci and Elias codes) pack arbitrary whole numbers into bitstreams with no separator symbols. We build an exact-integer Kirchhoff-Bellman conservation engine for prefix-code automata, proving exact Kraft-sum conservation, zero Hamming bias and a popcount-vs-length parity identity (an index shift between two defined potentials) for every $k$-bonacci code, an exact obstruction to lexicographic order-preservation, and compositional order-preservation for iterated Elias codes.
- **For Mathematicians:** For any finite-state prefix-code automaton `PrefixAut S`, we prove strict prefix-freeness (`accepts_prefix_free`) and build an exact-integer **Kirchhoff-Bellman Potential Engine** (`layer0_conservation`, `layer1_conservation`, `layer0_contraction`, `layer1_contraction`): local finite-state Kirchhoff equations over $\mathbb{Z}$ lift across all bit-lengths $N \in \mathbb{N}$ to exact conservation of Kraft sums, signed characters, and bit-cost potentials without real numbers or floating-point arithmetic. Applying the engine discharges four infinite families: (1) the **Order-Type $\omega$ Obstruction** (`order_type_omega_obstruction`), proving that any prefix automaton with a `0`-cycle reaching an accepting `1`-branch contains an infinite lexicographically descending chain and admits no order-preserving enumeration by $\mathbb{N}$; (2) **$k$-bonacci prefix automata** (`fibAut k`), proving exact Kraft conservation, exact zero Hamming bias (`fibAut_zero_bias`), and a **Jacobsthal popcount-vs-length parity identity** (`fibAut_parity_duality_embedding`, an index-shift identity between two potentials defined in `Defs.lean`); (3) **asymmetric-Kraft / symmetric-bias** run-length automata (`runAut_bias_symmetric`, symmetry of two explicit polynomials); and (4) a compositional **order-preserving Elias-lift tower** (`eliasLift_orderPreserving`; the $+1/2$ popcount-parity collapse across levels $r \ge 1$ is in `Theorems.lean`, not the Challenge).
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical prefix-coding & probability lineage:** Kraft (1949) and McMillan (1956) inequalities, Elias (1975) universal codes, Fraenkel & Klein (1996) Fibonacci codes, Guibas & Odlyzko (1981) string overlaps, Wald's identity and Feller (1968) (for zero-bias stopping-time intuition), and Levenshtein (1968) / Fenwick (2003) for Elias-$\delta$ / universal code length divergence. **Claimed contribution (not independently reviewed):** the unified exact-integer Kirchhoff-Bellman Potential Engine over leaky automata and signed characters, the $k$-bonacci Jacobsthal popcount-vs-length parity identity, the symmetry of the Hamming-bias polynomials of `runAut a b`, the order-type $\omega$ obstruction, and the compositional `eliasTower` $+1/2$ popcount-parity collapse. No prior record was found by the authors; the search was not recorded. The parity identity and the symmetry statement are short consequences of the definitions in `Defs.lean`.

### 96. [`dyadic-ldlt-schur-posdef-engine/`](dyadic-ldlt-schur-posdef-engine/) — **89 / 100**
- **Plain English:** To check that an infinite family of matrices is positive definite we need an argument that works for every size at once. We build an exact division-free $LDL ^ T$ elimination over dyadic rationals and an automaton that computes the pivots for all sizes from a finite recursion, then determine exactly when four families of van der Corput-type matrices are positive definite, including the sharp thresholds where they break.
- **For Mathematicians:** Over the exact ordered ring $\mathbb{Z}[1/2]$ of dyadic rationals (`Dyad`, mantissa and shift, decidable order, no reals; positive definiteness is stated for test vectors with dyadic entries, not real vectors) we prove: given an exact factorization $M = L D L ^ T$ with $L$ unit lower triangular, $M$ is strictly positive definite if and only if every pivot $D(r)$ is positive (`ldlt_strictlyPosDef_iff`), and a uniform pivot lower bound $\delta$ yields $v ^ T M v \ge \delta \sum _ r ((L ^ T v) _ r) ^ 2$ (`ldlt_quadForm_lower_bound`). Elimination commutes with taking leading blocks (`ldlt_prefix_restrict`). The infinite-dimensional engine is a **non-scalar Schur system** (`SchurSystem`, `block_schur_ldlt_step`, `schurSystem_nested_iff`): a finite state recursion produces the Schur pivots for dyadic block sizes $2 ^ k$ and, by prefix restriction, for every $n$, supporting scalar, anti-diagonal and rank-one boundary couplings. It decides four families built from the van der Corput radical inverse $\phi _ 2$: the Hamming family is positive definite for all $n$ iff $4 \beta \le 3 c$ with critical instance $(4, 3)$ and a sharp failure at $n = 16$ for $(15/4, 3)$ (`vdcHamming_*`); a weighted family with uniform pivot bound; a Gray-weighted family positive definite for all $n$ with pivot bounds $2/3$ and $5/8$ (`grayWeighted_*`); and an exponential family with critical ratio $7/6$ and a $2$-sparse negative witness at $n = 32$ (`expVdc_*`).
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical background:** plain $2 ^ {- \mathrm{pop}(x \oplus y)}$ is a Kronecker power of a $2 \times 2$ matrix, plain $2 ^ {- \lvert x - y \rvert}$ is the Kac-Murdock-Szego (1953) AR(1) Toeplitz covariance kernel (J. Rational Mech. Anal. 2, 767-800), and the base-2 van der Corput radical inverse is classical in quasi-Monte Carlo theory (Dick and Pillichshammer, *Digital Nets and Sequences*, Cambridge Univ. Press, 2010). **Contents (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search is recorded):** the division-free $\mathbb{Z}[1/2]$ $LDL ^ T$ iff engine, the non-scalar `SchurSystem` automaton and the four sharp-threshold families (Hamming, weighted, Gray-weighted, exponential).

### 102. [`carryless-collatz-2adic-gluing/`](carryless-collatz-2adic-gluing/) — **87 / 100**
- **Plain English:** The famous $3x+1$ Collatz map multiplies odd numbers by $3$, adds $1$, and divides by $2$. What happens if you run the exact same rule on a carryless binary multiplier (XOR instead of addition)? In the 2-adic numbers, we prove that carried $3x+1$ and carryless $3x+1$ are exact mirror images of each other — with a fast $O(k)$-bit decoder translating every parity trajectory back and forth — and we give a finite table checker that certifies this mirror property for arbitrary $2^r$-branch Collatz-like rules in one step. Yet turning carries on or off triggers a dramatic phase transition in "self-coding" numbers (numbers equal to their own parity trajectory): carryless maps freeze at $2^{v _ 2(p-1)}$, carried $a \equiv 1 \pmod 4$ maps collapse all the way to just $2$, while classical $3x+1$ produces infinitely many. Meanwhile, in ordinary integers, carryless $3x+1$ has $x = 1$ as its unique non-zero cycle.
- **For Mathematicians:** We build a three-layer pure-Init Lean 4 proof engine for 2-adic piecewise-affine and carryless-Collatz dynamics:

1. **Layer A — `ShiftFaithful` 2-Adic Conjugacy & $O(k)$ Hensel Inversion (`Syracuse_conjugacy`, `carried_carryless_3x1_syracuse_conjugate`, `periodic_orbit_classification`):** From two local bitwise axioms — 1-shift causality ($x \equiv y \pmod{2^{k+1}} \implies T(x) \equiv T(y) \pmod{2^k}$) and local bit-flipping ($T(x+2^k) \equiv T(x)+2^{k-1} \pmod{2^k}$) — the parity-vector encoder $Q _ T(k, x)$ and constructive $O(k)$-bit Hensel decoder $R _ T(k, y)$ establish exact 2-adic isometry, $m$-step Haar measure preservation, universal mutual conjugacy $\Phi _ {T _ 1 \to T _ 2} = R _ {T _ 2} \circ Q _ {T _ 1}$ with bit-exact Syracuse $v _ 2$-valuation preservation (in particular conjugating carried $3x+1$ to carryless $\mathrm{clmul}(3,x) \oplus 1$), and exact classification of projective 2-adic $p$-periodic orbits.
2. **Layer B — Exact `iff` Finite-Table Ultrametric Gluing Certifiers (`checkAffTable_iff_shiftFaithful`):** For any $r \ge 1$ and any $2^r$-branch carried or carryless piecewise-affine table $\tau$, a decidable finite Boolean checker inspecting only the $2^r$ residue classes is proved both necessary and sufficient (`↔`) for `ShiftFaithful` across all $k, x \in \mathbb{N}$.
3. **Layer C — Carry-vs-Carryless Self-Coding Phase Transition & Cycle Obstruction (`carryless_selfCoding_exact`, `carried_mod4_eq1_collapse`, `carryless_3x1_unique_nonzero_cycle`):** Counting $k$-bit self-coding residues $F _ T(k) = |\{x < 2^k : Q _ T(k, x) = x\}|$ reveals a sharp carry phase transition: carryless $T _ {p,1}^\oplus$ has exact count $2^{\min(k, v _ 2(p-1))}$, whereas a single binary carry collapses carried $T _ {a,1}^+$ ($a \equiv 1 \pmod 4$) to $F(k) = 2$ for all $k \ge 1$ (and $a \equiv 11 \pmod{16}$ to $F(k) = 6$ for $k \ge 3$), while classical $3x+1$ grows unboundedly ($F(k) \ge k+1$). Finally, an exact carryless trajectory bit-degree identity proves $x = 1$ is the unique non-zero cycle of carryless $3x+1$ in $\mathbb{N}$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Terras (1976), Lagarias (1985), Bernstein (1994), Anashin–Khrennikov (2009), Hicks et al. (2008); the iff 2^r-branch ultrametric gluing certifiers, universal binary degree-balance obstruction, and Carry-vs-Carryless Self-Coding Phase Transition are claimed here (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search was recorded).

### 105. [`gray-carry-whitening-transducer/`](gray-carry-whitening-transducer/) — **86 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Add two random $w$-bit numbers and look at the Gray code of the carry pattern. We prove that every possible value $z$ appears exactly $3 ^ {w - |z|}$ times, where $|z|$ is the number of ones in $z$, for every width at once.
- **For Mathematicians:** Let $G(n) = n \oplus (n \gg 1)$ be the reflected Gray code and $C$ the carry word of $a + b + c _ 0$. We prove the exact **whitening law**: for every width $w$ and every target $z < 2 ^ w$, the number of pairs $(a, b) \in [0, 2 ^ w) ^ 2$ with $G(C) \bmod 2 ^ w = z$ is $3 ^ {w - |z|}$, where $|z|$ is the popcount; the same count holds for the carry defect of Gray-code addition in Gray coordinates, and the untruncated parity-closed law on $[0, 2 ^ {w + 1})$ is `gray_carry_full_fiber_count`. For subtraction, the Gray carry and borrow words are **orthogonal**, $G(C) \mathbin{\&} G(B) = 0$ whenever $b + b _ 0 \le a$, with exact mask separation, an explicit bijection $\Phi _ {w, c _ 0, b _ 0}$ with a four-state inverse transducer, and the joint fiber law $[u \mathbin{\&} v = 0] \cdot 2 ^ {w - |u| - |v|}$. All counts come from one reusable engine: a finite-state transducer whose one-bit slice counts are balanced (`transducer_balanced_fiber_lift`, `radix_balanced_fiber_lift`) has product-form fiber counts at every width. In base $B$ the carry-difference word has fiber count $\binom{B + 1}{2} ^ {w - |z|} \binom{B}{2} ^ {|z|}$ (`radix_carry_whitening_law`); orthogonality of carry and borrow fails in every base $B \ge 3$ so $B = 2$ is unique, and for $k \ge 3$ operands the Gaussian-integer norm identity $D(k, 0) ^ 2 + D(k, 1) ^ 2 = 2 ^ {k + 1}$ gives unequal one-bit slice counts for the multi-operand carry transducer `multiCarrySlice1`, which blocks the balanced-fiber lifting argument used for $k = 2$ (`k_operand_whitening_impossibility`; this is only the slice-count inequality, not a proof that no balanced $k$-operand carry word exists).
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical background:** the reflected Gray code and its unit-step property (Gray 1953; Knuth, TAOCP Vol. 4A, 7.2.1.1). The four-state serial Gray-code adder already appears in **Lucal (1959)** and **Doran (2007, *The Gray Code*)**; `grayAddWith_eq_transducer4` is a Lean quotient-bisimulation mechanization of that adder generalized to carry-in, not a new state reduction. The single zero-carry count $3 ^ w$ is folklore (the $z = 0$ case). **Contents (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search is recorded):** the balanced-fiber and bijection lifting engine, carry-borrow Gray orthogonality with the bijection $\Phi$, the joint fiber law, the hypercube whitening law $3 ^ {w - |z|}$, its radix-$B$ generalization and the $k \ge 3$ slice-count obstruction.

### 106. [`carry-echo-sandpile-duality/`](carry-echo-sandpile-duality/) — **86 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Carry propagation in an adder is an Abelian sandpile: whenever a digit overflows, it topples chips to neighbouring columns. We build a purely constructive sandpile-group engine and solve two infinite families across every base $b \ge 2$ and width $w \ge 1$, giving an explicit closed-form formula for the sandpile identity, exact avalanche divisibility laws, and a Kummer carry conservation law.
- **For Mathematicians:** We formalize a reusable, purely constructive (`[propext, Quot.sound]`, zero `Classical.choice`) Abelian toppling and harmonic-group engine (`AbelianToppleSys`, `LinearBalance`, `HarmonicGroupCert`), proving confluence, odometer uniqueness (`confluent_and_odometer_unique`), burning necessity, and constructive group-isomorphism certification without invoking the matrix-tree theorem. We instantiate the engine on two parametric silicon sandpile families for all bases $b \ge 2$ and widths $w \ge 1$: (1) the **Carry-Echo sandpile** (each toppling sends $1$ chip up and $b$ chips down, echoing carries), proving exact skew-weight conservation modulo the repunit $R _ {b, w + 1} = (b ^ {w + 1} - 1)/(b - 1)$, exact quadratic-harmonic potential drop $\Delta \Psi = (\sum f _ i)(b ^ {w + 1} - 1)$ (`carryEcho_total_avalanche_law`), local dual-skew recurrence classification and the right-inverse half of a bijection with $\mathrm{Fin}(R _ {b, w + 1})$ (the converse half is in the library, not in `Challenge.lean`), and the **explicit closed-form Myers greedy identity** $e _ {b, w}$ (`carryEcho_explicitIdentity_eq`) with universal self-addition avalanche laws (`carryEcho_identity_laws_ge2`); and (2) the **Directed-Carry sandpile**, where every stable configuration is recurrent and chip mass satisfies exact Kummer conservation (`directedCarry_kummer_conservation`).
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical sandpile & chip-firing lineage:** Dhar's Abelian sandpile burning algorithm (1990), **Björner, Lovász & Shor** (*Chip-firing games on graphs*, EJC 1991) for confluence and odometer uniqueness, and **Lee, Liang & Tzeng** (1991) together with **Speer** (*Asymmetric Abelian Sandpile Models*, J. Stat. Phys. 1993, §4–5) for the 1D $(1\text{ up}, b\text{ down})$ asymmetric sandpile's recurrent count $(b ^ {w + 1} - 1)/(b - 1)$ and forbidden-subconfiguration characterization. **Contributions claimed here (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search was recorded):** the purely constructive (`[propext, Quot.sound]`, zero `Classical.choice`, zero matrix-tree theorem) odometer-cancellation burning engine reused across both families, the explicit Myers greedy-encoder closed-form identity $e _ {b, w}$, the universal self-addition avalanche laws, and the exact constant-drop divisibility $(b ^ {w + 1} - 1) \mid \Delta \Psi$.

### 112. [`arx-fibonacci-isospectrality/`](arx-fibonacci-isospectrality/) — **84 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Mixing a secret $m$-bit XOR key $c$ into a modular Fibonacci counter $(x, y) \to (y,\; x + (y \oplus c)) \bmod 2^m$ cannot change a single cycle length — every single key produces the exact same loop sizes as unkeyed Fibonacci, though changing $x$ to $3x$ breaks it immediately on 2-bit numbers.
- **For Mathematicians:** Exact finite-level 2-adic perturbation law $((y + 2^r) \oplus c) \equiv (y \oplus c) + \sigma _ r \cdot 2^r \pmod{2^{r+2}}$ ($\sigma _ r \in \{1, 3\}$) and linearized Jacobian lifting prove that every non-fixed $(x, y)$ has exact minimal period $3 \cdot 2^{m - 1 - r _ 0}$, making $(x, y) \mapsto (x \oplus c, y \oplus c)$ an exact period-preserving involution (though not a conjugacy) between $G _ {c,m}$ and $G _ {0,m}$, with a sharp $m = 2$ failure witness for $3x + (y \oplus c)$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Builds on 2-adic differentiability of XOR (Anashin, Uniformly distributed sequences of p-adic integers, II, arXiv:math/0209407 (2002); Anashin–Khrennikov 2009), T-functions (Klimov–Shamir 2002/2004), and unkeyed Pisano periods (Wall 1960); no prior record of key-invariant ARX Fibonacci cycle spectra was found by the authors; the search was not recorded. ### 114. [`modular-carry-residue-duality/`](modular-carry-residue-duality/) — **84 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Dividing a binary number by an odd number can be done by long division from the top bit or by a carry circuit from the bottom bit. Running both on the same input makes the count of 1-bits in the input cancel exactly, for numbers of every length and every odd divisor. For divisors of the form $2 ^ k - 1$ and $2 ^ k + 1$ the number of 1-bits in the 2-adic reciprocal $r / m$ depends only on the length and the trailing zeros of $r$, not on its other bits.
- **For Mathematicians:** Bidirectional dual-transducer coupling: an MSB Euclidean remainder automaton and an LSB 2-adic division carry transducer over the same state space $\mathrm{Fin}\,m$ carry opposite-sign one-bit coboundary certificates whose coupling cancels $s _ 2(n)$, giving the master law $m \cdot \mathcal{I} _ m(n, L) = 2 ((n \bmod m) + c _ L) - O _ m(n \bmod m, L)$ for every odd $m$, with synchronized-trace divisibility and carry complementarity. Mersenne rotation orbits and negacyclic antipodes give residue-blind laws $s _ 2(Q _ {t k}(r)) = t (k - s _ 2(r)) + 1 - v _ 2(r)$ and $s _ 2(Q _ {2 t d}(r)) = t d + 1 - v _ 2(r)$ with exact exception set $t d = 2$ and kernel-checked sharpness.
- **Prior art & stack:** Classical: base-$2 ^ k$ digit-sum congruences, Kummer carry identity, MSB Euclidean division automaton, LSB Hensel / Montgomery exact division, Newman Thue-Morse bias. The negacyclic reciprocal popcount law is the 2-adic shadow of generalized Midy theorem (Midy 1836; Ginsberg 2004; Lewittes 2007), derived here from the dual coboundary engine. The dual MSB/LSB coupling and Mersenne/Fermat conservation laws are claimed by the authors as new (not independently reviewed; no prior record was found by the authors and the search was not recorded). The authors replayed both reciprocal popcount laws on small ranges with 0 mismatches; this proves nothing beyond those ranges.

### 115. [`kummer-carry-valuation-automata/`](kummer-carry-valuation-automata/) — **84 / 100**
- **Plain English:** When two binary numbers are added, carries ripple from digit to digit, and Kummer proved that the number of carries tells you exactly how many times 2 divides a certain huge counting number (a binomial coefficient). This package turns that into a small finite-table machine that reads digits one at a time; checking the table once by computer yields a statement about every number, however long. With it the package proves exact laws for binomial coefficients at bit-shifted and Gray-coded arguments and ceilings (slopes 5/2, 8/3, 13/4) for choosing n out of 11n, 25n and 55n; the Challenge statements give the 5/2 upper bound for 11n and show the 13/4 bound is attained on an explicit family for 55n.
- **For Mathematicians:** Pure-Init Lean 4 development of a Carry-Valuation Automaton engine: Legendre, Kummer and trinomial Kummer proved from scratch; finite-state automata (CVA S) with decide-checkable coboundary, tropical lower/upper-potential and doubling-invariant positive-input lifting theorems and a zero-slack lasso pumping theorem certifying exact sharpness; exact shifted-XOR/OR/AND Kummer collapse laws and the trinomial law; Gray-code binomial universal evenness and the strict sandwich v2 binom(n + G(n), n) + 1 <= v2 binom(n + (n XOR 2n), n); a parametric 2(k-1)-state engine for binom(k n, n) with slope bounds 5/2 (k = 11), 8/3 (k = 25), 13/4 (k = 55); the Challenge statements prove the upper bound for k = 11 and equality on an explicit infinite family for k = 55.
- **Prior art & stack:** Legendre (1808), Kummer (1852); carry/digit-sum automata after Karp (1978) and Heuberger-Prodinger are the closest prior art for the automaton viewpoint; the Kummer identity is also in Mathlib. Not independently reviewed; no prior-art search is recorded. Contents: zero-slack lasso pumping lemma (equality on an explicit infinite family), shifted XOR/OR/AND Kummer collapse laws (instances of Kummer's theorem), Gray-code binomial evenness and strict sandwich, and slope bounds 5/2, 8/3, 13/4 for v2 of binom(k n, n) at k = 11, 25, 55.

### 116. [`two-regular-gray-defect-cones/`](two-regular-gray-defect-cones/) — **84 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Gray code is a way of counting in binary so consecutive numbers differ in only one bit. If you compare the 1-bits of Gray code against ordinary binary and keep a running total up to any number n, individual steps can be arbitrarily negative, yet the running total never drops below zero and never exceeds 2/5 of n, hitting that exact 2/5 ceiling infinitely often. This package builds a reusable compiler for 2-regular binary matrix machines and proves that tiny exact-integer matrix certificates checked by Lean's kernel automatically guarantee infinite inequalities, modular parity laws, parabolic bounds, and exact identities for every whole number n.
- **For Mathematicians:** Formalizes a pure-Init 2-regular linear-representation algebra compiler (`LinRep d` with leading-zero stability $M _ 0 v = v$; closed under `const`, `scale`, `add`, `sub`, Kronecker `mul`, `even`/`odd`, `shift1`, and `prefixSum`) paired with four finite exact-integer matrix certificate engines (`IdentityCert`, `IntertwineCert`, `ModCert`/`checkModCert`, and `ConeCert`/`ConePosCert`/`checkConePosCert` with integer scaling denominators $\alpha, \beta > 0$ and positive-domain entry $P M _ 1 v \ge 0$). Instantiates the engine on the 1-parameter base-$q$ Gray-Binary defect prefix sum $S _ q(q, n)$ (specializing to switching-weight excess $W _ G(n)$ at $q = 1$ and value excess $E _ G(n)$ at $q = 2$), proving parametric nonnegativity ($q \ge 0$), the sharp parity-coupled $2/5$ bound $5 W _ G(n) \le 2n - 2 + (n \bmod 2)$ with infinite extremal attainment (`extremal_W_G_odd`/`even`), the aperiodic odd-filtered Thue-Morse parity law for $W _ G(n) \bmod 2$, the period-8 law for $E _ G(n) \bmod 4$, the exact dyadic parabolic sandwich $Q _ {\mathrm{dyadic}}(n) \le 2 E _ G(n) \le 2 Q _ {\mathrm{dyadic}}(n)$, and the $q$-independent second-difference law $S _ q(q, 2n) + S _ q(q, 2n+2) - 2 S _ q(q, 2n+1) = -2(n \bmod 2)$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Linear representations of k-regular sequences are due to Allouche and Shallit (1992); automatic decision tools such as Walnut (Mousavi 2016) decide bounded automatic predicates via DFAs, while classical binary sum-of-digits envelopes use Trollope-Delange (1968, 1975) Takagi analysis. The pure-Init 2-regular algebra compiler coupled with exact integer-scaled polyhedral invariant-cone and modular-subspace certifiers (`ConePosCert`, `ModCert`) and the five Gray-Binary defect theorems (2/5 ceiling, aperiodic Thue-Morse parity, period-8 mod-4 law, dyadic parabolic sandwich, and q-independent second-difference law) are claimed by the authors as new; not independently reviewed, no prior-art search is recorded.

### 117. [`f2-rank-bitwise-matrix-automata/`](f2-rank-bitwise-matrix-automata/) — **84 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Determinants say nothing about matrices that are singular, so for bit-patterned matrices over the two-element field we prove exact formulas for the rank and the number of solutions of $M x = 0$ for every size $n$, not just powers of two. For example the parity matrix of carry-free sums has nullity $\mathrm{popcount}(\mathrm{gray}(n - 1)) - 1$ and is invertible exactly when $n$ is a power of two.
- **For Mathematicians:** Most bitwise linear-algebra theorems concern sizes $2 ^ k$ or determinants, which vanish identically on singular matrices and carry no information about rank or corank. For arbitrary $n \times n$ matrices over $\mathbb{F} _ 2$ we build a block-elimination engine (`HasRank`, `LinEquiv`, `hasRank_schur_inv`) proving rank uniqueness (`hasRank_unique`) and the exact kernel-count law $|\ker M| = 2 ^ {n - r}$ (`hasRank_kerCount`), plus an **isotropic rank-one decoupling** theorem: a symmetric block $A$ with an isotropic preimage $x$ of $u$ makes the coupled block matrix of rank $\mathrm{rank}(A) + \mathrm{rank}(B)$ (`hasRank_isotropic_rank1_decouple`). Applications for all $n$: the carry-free XOR-sum parity matrix $M _ 0 (i, j) = \mathrm{popcount}((i + j) \mathbin{\&} (i \oplus j)) \bmod 2$ has nullity exactly $\mathrm{popcount}(\mathrm{gray}(n - 1)) - 1$ for $n \ge 2$, hence exactly $2 ^ {\mathrm{popcount}(\mathrm{gray}(n - 1)) - 1}$ kernel vectors, and is invertible if and only if $n = 2 ^ k$ with $k \ge 1$ (`M0_gray_nullity`, `M0_kerCount`, `M0_invertible_iff_pow2`); a Hamming-type family $H _ d$ has kernel size $2 ^ {\mathrm{hcNullity}(d, n)}$ and $H _ 0$ is invertible iff the base-4 digit DFA accepts $n$ (`H_kerCount`, `H0_invertible_iff_base4`).
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical background:** Kronecker, Walsh-Hadamard and Sierpinski-type bit matrices are classical for sizes $2 ^ k$; rank over $\mathbb{F} _ 2$ by Gaussian elimination and the Schur complement rank formula (Guttman 1946; Haynsworth 1968) are standard. Gray-code popcount identities are classical (Knuth, TAOCP Vol. 4A). **Contents (not independently reviewed; no prior-art search is recorded):** the exact rank, nullity and kernel-cardinality engine for arbitrary non-power-of-two $n$, the isotropic rank-one decoupling, the exact Gray-popcount nullity of $M _ 0$ and the base-4 DFA invertibility criterion for $H _ 0$.

### 118. [`walsh-mobius-runs-dihedral-spectra/`](walsh-mobius-runs-dihedral-spectra/) — **84 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Matrices built from bitwise XOR or AND of row and column indices can be diagonalized exactly by the Walsh-Hadamard and Mobius transforms. We prove this for every size $2 ^ k$ at once, show the transforms of any finite-automaton sequence are again finite-automaton sequences of the same size, and use that to get exact closed-form determinants for a whole family of run-counting matrices.
- **For Mathematicians:** For $f : \mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{Z}$, the XOR-convolution matrix $C _ f (x, y) = f(x \oplus y)$ on $[0, 2 ^ k)$ is diagonalized by the Walsh-Hadamard transform, and we prove $\det C _ f = \prod _ u \mathrm{wht} _ k (f)(u)$ and $\det(\lambda I - C _ f) = \prod _ u (\lambda - \mathrm{wht} _ k (f)(u))$ with a purely integer Leibniz determinant (`det_xorConv`, `charPoly_xorConv`). Likewise the AND-lattice (meet) matrix $g(x \mathbin{\&} y)$ has determinant equal to the product of the Mobius down-transform values (`det_andLattice`). The main construction is a **bit-automaton transfer compiler** (a standard automaton construction) (`wht_transfer_compiler`, `mobiusDown_transfer_compiler`, `scaledDihedral_collapse`): if $f$ is computed by a $d$-state bit-automaton then so is its Walsh transform, with a state count independent of $k$. Applied to the runs kernel (`runs` counts the maximal runs of 1-bits; a definition counting all runs does not satisfy the formula) this yields the closed-form spectrum $\prod _ u (1 - q) ^ r \cdot \mathrm{pellQ}(q, k + 1 - 2 r)$ (`det_runs_xorConv`), singularity at $q = -1$ for every odd exponent (`runs_xorConv_neg_one_singular_odd`), unimodularity $\det = -1$ of the two-runs AND-lattice for all $k \ge 2$ (`det_two_runs_andLattice_unimodular`), and a proof that the runs AND-lattice is never positive definite (`runs_andLattice_never_posdef`).
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical foundations:** diagonalization of XOR-convolution (dyadic circulant) matrices by Sylvester-Walsh-Hadamard matrices (Sylvester 1867; Walsh 1923), and determinant factorization of meet matrices $g(x \wedge y)$ on finite posets via the zeta-matrix congruence and Mobius function, the Smith-Lindstrom-Wilf theorem (Smith 1875; Wilf 1968; Lindstrom 1969). **Novelty:** the authors did not find the bit-automaton Walsh and Mobius transfer compilers, the scaled-dihedral collapse certificate, the closed-form runs-kernel spectrum, the odd-dimension singularity or the unimodular two-runs AND-lattice in the sources above; no search was recorded and the claim is not independently reviewed. The determinant formulas for XOR-convolution and AND-lattice matrices are classical; the transfer compiler is a standard automaton construction.

### 129. [`signed-digit-naf-lookahead/`](signed-digit-naf-lookahead/) — **82 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Computers can write a number using the digits -1, 0 and +1 instead of only 0 and 1, which lets them add two numbers in constant depth without waiting for carries to ripple across a million digits and store numbers with fewer non-zero digits. This package proves, for numbers of any length, that a small 5-state machine always finds the minimum-weight Non-Adjacent Form with an exact step-by-step weight-saving ledger, counts the zero-saving inputs by exact cubic recurrences, and builds a local 4-digit odd-lookahead parallel adder that never increases total Hamming weight. It also proves that no causal (zero-lookahead) carry-free parallel adder of any finite lookback radius can be weight-subadditive, and that classical Chow-Robertson addition suffers an explicit 3/2 weight blowup even when adding zero.
- **For Mathematicians:** Formalizes a pure-data finite-state signed-digit carry-absorption certificate compiler (`SDTransducer m k`, zero `Prop` fields) whose finite Boolean transition certificates (`checkValCert`, `checkDefectCert`, `checkRangeCert`, `checkZeroFlushCert`) lift unconditionally to arbitrary-length signed-digit streams: value conservation, exact additive weight-defect conservation with pointwise non-negative edge defect, and signed-digit range safety. Instantiates the engine on (1) the 5-state canonical SD-to-NAF recoder (`nat_naf_conservation`, `nafRecode_minimal_iff`, `naf_global_minimal`, `naf_unique_wt`) and extracts two exact cubic zero-defect counting recurrences (`countZeroDefectBin_recurrence` with characteristic polynomial $\lambda^3 - \lambda^2 - 2\lambda + 1$ and `countZeroDefectSD_recurrence` with $\lambda^3 - \lambda^2 - 3\lambda + 1$), and (2) the 25-state Odd-Lookahead Carry-Free Parallel Adder (`olAdd_sound_and_subadditive`, `olParallelEval_eq_transducer`, `olAdd_naf_fixed`, `addTree_conservation`) with a strictly local 4-digit parallel window. Proves sharpness via universal zero-lookahead impossibility across all finite causal lookback radii $r \in \mathbb{N}$ (`causal_adder_not_subadditive`) and the explicit $3/2$ periodic weight blowup of classical Chow-Robertson ($r = 1$) on $(1, 1, 0)^m + 0$ (`chowRobertson_periodic_blowup`), with kernel-checked non-vacuity and rejection of deliberately wrong certificates.

### 131. [`gray-basis-feistel-carry-orbits/`](gray-basis-feistel-carry-orbits/) — **82 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Mixing a bit permutation with binary `+ 1` usually produces chaotic cycle lengths, but doing the permutation in the binary Gray-code basis completely tames the dynamics at every bit width. Reversing bits in Gray basis and adding 1 collapses so strongly that every number returns to itself in at most 4 steps for every width, yet adding a single top bit (`+ 2^(w-1) + 1`) turns the exact same map into a single full-period loop visiting every w-bit number once. - **Prior art & stack:** Binary Gray code and bit-permutation + carry-increment orbit analysis (distinct from standard-basis bitrev-increment-cycles). Not independently reviewed; no prior-art search is recorded. ### 143. [`binary-srs-matrix-cp-compiler/`](binary-srs-matrix-cp-compiler/) — **81 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Imagine a machine that rewrites a string of 0s and 1s with a short list of find-and-replace rules, and you want to be sure that for every string, however long, it always stops and always ends at the same final string no matter which rule you apply first. Instead of testing infinitely many strings, give the Lean kernel a small certificate (a few little tables of whole numbers that act like a score that must drop at every step, plus a finite list showing how every possible rule clash resolves). If that finite check passes, the guarantee holds for every bit-string. As showpieces it builds tiny local rule sets that compute remainders after dividing by 3, by 2^m - 1 and by 2^m + 1 for every size m, and it pins down exactly when a family of growing rules stays well behaved: precisely when a divisibility condition on powers of b holds.
- **For Mathematicians:** A pure-Init reflection compiler for binary string rewriting: a pure-data ConfluenceCert (layered natural-number matrix weights with positive corner entries, stacked lexicographically via a constructive well-founded order on fixed-length vectors, plus complete critical-pair enumeration with deterministic (ruleIdx, pos) trace replay) is checked by kernel decide, and the master theorem cert_sound lifts it to termination, global confluence and unique normal forms on all bit-strings (constructive Newman lemma), with a verified Church-Rosser normalizer deciding convertibility. It proves the exact Euler-Fermat confluence dichotomy Confluent(S(b, r, s, k, d)) iff k <= b^s and d | b^r - b^s for the length-increasing family {10 -> 0^b 1, 0^(k+d) -> 0^k, 1^r -> 1^s} (terminating by one 2x2 matrix despite exponential derivations), a semantic-invariant confluence meta-theorem, and infinite length-preserving Mersenne (2^m - 1, m >= 2) and Fermat (2^m + 1, m >= 1) local normalizer families computing the residue on arbitrary-width bit-strings, plus semantic completeness of a 4-rule mod-3 system, with kernel-checked sharpness and certificate-mutation rejections.
- **Prior art & stack:** Matrix interpretations for string rewriting termination: Hofbauer-Waldmann (RTA 2006) and Endrullis-Waldmann-Zantema (2008); critical pairs and Newman's lemma: Knuth-Bendix (1970), Newman (1942); certified checkers in Isabelle/HOL (IsaFoR/CeTA) and Coq (CoLoR/Rainbow). The mod-3 system has one redundant rule (sharpness of dropping each rule is kernel-checked in the package). What may be new (not independently checked; no search was recorded by the authors): a zero-dependency pure-Init reflection compiler with materialized arbitrary-dimension matrix stacks and trace-replay critical-pair verifier, the 5-parameter Euler-Fermat confluence dichotomy, and the parametric Mersenne/Fermat local normalizer families. An independent novelty review located the certified checkers above (IsaFoR/CeTA, CoLoR) and Hofbauer-Waldmann as prior work and located no prior statement of the dichotomy or the mod-3 normalizer; that search was shallow and is not evidence of novelty.

### 144. [`bitwise-grundy-periodicity-compiler/`](bitwise-grundy-periodicity-compiler/) — **80 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Two players take turns removing stones from a pile, but a removal is only allowed if the binary digits of the subtraction behave in a certain way, such as needing no borrowing or not raising the number of 1-bits. For rules of the first kind the winning pattern repeats from the very start with a power-of-two period, for every pile size at once. For other rules, a small checker examines a short finite table and, when it accepts, Lean concludes the exact pattern for every pile size and for any collection of piles. One game, whose rule looks at the parity of the borrow count, provably has a repeating one-pile pattern but a never-repeating two-pile pattern.
- **For Mathematicians:** Subtraction games whose move legality is a guard on the borrow/carry structure of n - s (borrow-free, at most B borrows, popcount delta, mask tests, boolean combinations). Proved: the Kummer-type identity popcount(n - s) + popcount(s) = popcount(n) + borrowCount(n, s); exact 2^(k+B)-periodicity of borrow-budget guards; the dyadic borrow-wall theorem (every wall-guarded game is purely periodic from n = 0 with period 2^L); the full multi-heap Sprague-Grundy XOR theorem; a kernel-checked certificate compiler checkCert whose acceptance yields grundy G n = evalPeriodic c n for all n, with minimal period, winning bitmask and winning-move oracle; an odd-period (5) showcase game despite a dyadic gate; and a provably aperiodic borrow-parity game with a periodic 1-heap but aperiodic 2-heap winning set. Pure Lean 4 Init, zero Mathlib.
- **Prior art & stack:** Classical subtraction games (Golomb 1966; Guy and Smith 1956; Berlekamp, Conway and Guy, Winning Ways 1982; Fraenkel numeration-system and aperiodic subtraction games) with state-independent move sets and pigeonhole periodicity; the Sprague-Grundy XOR theorem (Sprague 1935, Grundy 1939); Kummer's carry theorem. The borrow-count localization, the dyadic borrow-wall theorem, the wall-breaching phase transition and the periodic-1-heap versus aperiodic-2-heap split of the borrow-parity game appear new to the authors. No prior record was found by the authors; the search was not recorded, and this is a heuristic claim, not an exhaustive survey. Not independently reviewed.

### 145. [`ca-torsion-lyapunov-flux-compiler/`](ca-torsion-lyapunov-flux-compiler/) — **80 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Imagine a ring of on/off light bulbs of any length that all update at once by peeking at their neighbours. Give the Lean kernel a small local checklist (a score for each tiny pattern plus a boundary correction) and, if every tiny pattern passes, it proves a statement about rings of every length forever: some rules never change a pattern count, some always settle or lock into a gliding wave within a fixed multiple of the ring length, and two rules conserve a count only modulo 3 or modulo 2, a hidden clock-arithmetic quantity with no ordinary integer version.
- **For Mathematicians:** A pure-Init reflection compiler for 1D binary cellular automata: a pure-data CACert (potential table phi, flux/gauge table psi, window M) checked by kernel decide on 2^M windows lifts to all ring lengths w >= 1 via a telescoping Gauss identity (valid even for w < M). It discharges exact conservation, Z/dZ torsion conservation with no integer lift (Pivato-style flux cocycles), strict shift-equivariant Hamming dissipation with linear-time fixed-point termination, global acyclicity and traveling-wave locking, and, through a verified two-step rule compiler (k = 3 to k = 5), blinker convergence and absence of odd cycles for ECA 108. A Bellman-Ford obstruction shows the flux psi is essential.
- **Prior art & stack:** Hattori-Takesue 1991 and Boccara-Fuks 1998/2002 (additive and number-conserving laws); Pivato, Nonlinearity 15 (2002), conservation laws valued in abelian groups via local flux cocycles (the torsion mode mechanizes its finite Z/dZ case); Goles-Martinez 1990 (Lyapunov functionals); Wolfram 1984 and Kurka 2003 (ECA 108 and 152 benchmarks). What this package adds (not independently reviewed): a unified kernel-checked pure-data certificate compiler with flux-mediated strict dissipation bounds and concrete nonlinear k = 4 torsion rules. Each theorem concerns one fixed rule table (for example 0xac8c or ECA 108), for every ring length; none is a statement about all rules.

### 146. [`tfunction-curvature-holonomy/`](tfunction-curvature-holonomy/) — **80 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Think of a counter whose next value is computed by a circuit where each output bit only looks at lower-order input bits. It makes a good pseudo-random generator only if, starting from 0, it visits every n-bit value exactly once before repeating, for every word size n. This package proves exact rules for when a full cycle at n bits stretches to n + 1 bits, shows that for whole families of circuits a check on just 16 values (4 bits) settles every word size, and shows that for other families no fixed word size is ever enough.
- **For Mathematicians:** Pure-Init Lean 4 development of the curvature-holonomy transport law for single-cycle 1-Lipschitz T-functions: an exact bit-slice doubling criterion (unit slice derivative plus odd holonomy), the identity D_{w+1}(f) = 2 D_w(f) + K_w(f) turning holonomy into a curvature-sum congruence mod 2^{w+2}, a finite-to-infinite lifting engine from a width-4 check, involution and F_2-linear curvature annihilators, the universal carry-mask XOR symmetry, a complete 4-bit characterization of a 4-parameter hybrid ARX family (independent of k and of high bits of C), and the complete classification of the run family R_{d,C} (single-cycle at all widths iff C_0 = C_d = 1) with sharp failure at width d+1, yielding the no-universal-finite-threshold theorem.
- **Prior art & stack:** Klimov-Shamir single-cycle T-functions (CHES 2002, FSE 2004); Anashin-Khrennikov 2-adic ergodicity (Applied Algebraic Dynamics, 2009); Larin mod-8 criterion for integer polynomials (2002). Not independently reviewed; no novelty search was recorded. The authors' claimed contributions: an integer curvature-holonomy transport law, a universal carry-mask XOR symmetry, a complete 4-bit hybrid classification, the d-run family classification with failure at width d + 1, and the no-universal-finite-threshold theorem for 1-Lipschitz T-functions.

### 147. [`two-group-ryser-permanent-congruences/`](two-group-ryser-permanent-congruences/) — **80 / 100**
- **Plain English:** The permanent of a matrix is like the determinant without the minus signs and is notoriously hard to compute. For matrices with a large built-in symmetry by a group of $2 ^ k$ elements, we prove the permanent is determined modulo $2 ^ {k + 1}$ by a simple count of odd solutions, with sharper statements for XOR-circulants and for hypercube adjacency matrices.
- **For Mathematicians:** We first formalize the bitmask Ryser inclusion-exclusion formula $\mathrm{perm}(A) = \mathrm{ryserSum}(A)$ over all subsets encoded as bit masks (`perm_eq_ryserSum`). We then prove a **2-group orbit-collapse** divisibility principle (`twoGroup_sum_dvd`): if a group $G$ of order $2 ^ k$ acts on a finite set $X$ and $\Delta$ is $G$-invariant with $2 ^ {s + c} \mid \Delta(x)$ whenever the stabilizer of $x$ has order $2 ^ s$, then $2 ^ {k + c} \mid \sum _ x \Delta(x)$. Applied to the transversal-sum expansion of the permanent this gives, for any matrix equivariant under a free $2 ^ k$-group action, $\mathrm{perm}(A) \equiv \#\{\text{odd solutions}\} \pmod {2 ^ {k + 1}}$ (`perm_mod_two_pow_succ`, `xorCirc_perm_mod_two_pow_succ`), divisibility by $2 ^ {k + 1}$ for XOR-circulants with even row sum when $k \ge 3$ (`xorCirc_even_rowSum_dvd`), an explicit congruence modulo $2 ^ {k + 2}$ for odd row sum (`xorCirc_odd_mod_two_pow_add_two`), and exact laws for the hypercube $Q _ k$: $\mathrm{perm} \equiv 1 + 2 ^ k (k - 1) \pmod {2 ^ {k + 2}}$ for odd $k$, $2 ^ {k + 1} \mid \mathrm{perm}$ for even $k \ge 4$, and $2 ^ {m + 1} \mid \mathrm{perm}(I + A(Q _ m))$ for odd $m \ge 3$, where $I + A(Q _ m)$ is the $2 ^ m \times 2 ^ m$ biadjacency matrix of $Q _ {m+1}$ (the permanent is $272$ for $m = 3$).
- **Prior art & stack:** **Classical background:** Ryser's inclusion-exclusion formula (Ryser 1963); permanents modulo powers of two and Valiant's hardness of the permanent (Valiant 1979); Kummer-style 2-adic orbit arguments for group actions; permanents of hypercube adjacency matrices relate to counts of perfect matchings and 2-factors of $Q _ k$. **Novelty:** the authors did not find the 2-group Ryser orbit-collapse engine, the mod $2 ^ {k + 2}$ XOR-circulant defect law or the hypercube permanent congruences in the sources above; no search was recorded and the claim is not independently reviewed.

### 148. [`fcsr-cyclotomic-zolotarev/`](fcsr-cyclotomic-zolotarev/) — **79 / 100**
- **Plain English:** A feedback-with-carry shift register is a tiny hardware circuit: bits shift one place each tick, a feedback adds some of them together, and a small carry is kept in memory like an odometer. This package proves, for every odd wiring number q and every register size, that one exact integer captures the whole circuit state so its output is the binary expansion of a fraction with denominator q, and derives exact guarantees such as bounded carry memory, exact balance of bits and carries over a cycle, and, when q leaves remainder 3 or 5 on division by 8, a cycle length that is always even.
- **For Mathematicians:** Pure-Init Lean 4 development of the exact 2-adic potential P_q(a, m) of Fibonacci FCSRs of every odd connection integer q >= 3, a global bijection conjugating the clock tick to p -> (p - q (p mod 2))/2, with the exact word representation q W_n + 2^n P(step^n s) = P(s), bijectivity on the periodic core, constructive Goresky-Klapper carry bounds via a complement involution, universal carry-bit conservation over periodic orbits, half-period complementation certificates (q | (2^h + 1) P), the d-section cyclotomic engine (q | repunit(d, k) gives section quantum c(t) in [1, d-1], period k), a from-scratch inversion-parity Zolotarev proof that 2^T = 1 mod q forces T even for q = 3, 5 mod 8 (prime or composite), and infinite families such as q = 2^k + 3.
- **Prior art & stack:** FCSR theory (Klapper-Goresky 1997; Arnault-Berger); Zolotarev lemma (1872) and the Jacobi symbol for q = 3, 5 mod 8 (classical, not claimed new). Not independently reviewed; no prior-art search is recorded, so "possibly new" means only that the authors did not locate these statements. Possibly new: exact integer potential bijection with constructive carry bounds, half-period and d-section cyclotomic conservation laws on the full state, from-scratch inversion-parity proof of the even-period law, infinite q = 2^k +- const families.

### 149. [`two-adic-newton-poly-optimality/`](two-adic-newton-poly-optimality/) — **79 / 100**
- **Plain English:** To compute $1/a$ modulo $2^N$ for odd $a$ one can use Newton's iteration, or a fixed polynomial in $a$, or an arbitrary arithmetic circuit. We prove exact optimality laws for all three: the best precision a polynomial of degree $D$ can reach is exactly $2(D+1) - \mathrm{popcount}(D+1)$ bits, and no circuit with multiplicative depth $k$ beats $2^{k+1}$ bits (a circuit here is a straight-line integer program counting products, not a model of mod-$2^N$ hardware arithmetic).
- **For Mathematicians:** Exact two-sided law $N \le 2(D+1) - \mathrm{pop}(D+1)$ for degree-$D$ polynomial 2-adic reciprocals, a universal multiplicative-depth ceiling $2^{k+1}$ for integer circuits, exact valuation orbits for reciprocal and inverse-square-root Newton steps (cubic popcount super-gain), certified from finite affine contraction certificates.
- **Prior art & stack:** Hensel lifting and Newton iteration (Montgomery 1985); Dumas (2014) seed (see also Dumas, [arXiv:1209.6626](https://arxiv.org/abs/1209.6626)); Legendre factorial valuation; Bhargava (1997) generalized factorials (nearest prior art for the odd-unit threshold). The threshold $2(D+1) - \mathrm{pop}(D+1)$ is the 2-adic valuation of $(2D+2)!$ and the sequence is [OEIS A005187](https://oeis.org/A005187), so the polynomial law may be known in substance. The depth ceiling $2^{k+1}$ and the cubic and quartic inverse-square-root laws were not found in the sources above; no search was recorded. Not independently reviewed.

### 150. [`carryless-carried-resonance-curve/`](carryless-carried-resonance-curve/) — **78 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Multiplying numbers with carries (the ordinary way) and without carries (the way a binary polynomial multiplier over $\mathbb{F} _ 2$ does) give different answers. We prove exactly how many trailing binary digits the two answers share for the powers $a^n$ in three regimes, and find an exact "resonance curve" (a further case, treated through a perturbed bridge) where the ordinary and carry-free worlds interlock.
- **For Mathematicians:** Exact closed-form valuation law in three regimes (I, II, III) for $v _ 2(a^n - \mathrm{clpow}(a, n))$ comparing carried and carry-less ($\mathbb{F} _ 2[x]$) powers, an infinitely inhabited resonance curve where the bridge is a scaled 2-adic isometry with a unique non-natural root, and a reusable Hensel-bridge lifter.
- **Prior art & stack:** Carry-less multiplication (CLMUL, Gueron-Kounavis 2010) and Frobenius dilation; Hensel lifting and 2-adic isometries (Anashin-Khrennikov 2009). No prior record of the valuation law or the resonance-curve isometry was found by the authors; the search was not recorded. The root-shift law is proved only for bit $0$ of the root. Not independently reviewed.

### 151. [`stern-walsh-carry-resonance/`](stern-walsh-carry-resonance/) — **78 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Stern's diatomic sequence $s(n)$ (1, 1, 2, 1, 3, 2, 3, 1, 4, ...) counts hyperbinary representations and sits at the heart of the Calkin-Wilf tree of all positive fractions. We prove that every one of its $2^k$ Walsh-Hadamard coefficients at level $k$ is a signed pure power of $3$ with an explicit exponent, for every $k$ at once, and that adding a carry shift inside the Thue-Morse sign turns the level sums into an exact period-4 or affine pattern decided by $c$ modulo $3$.
- **For Mathematicians:** Exact Walsh-Hadamard spectrum of Stern's diatomic sequence ($\pm 3^e$ with Gray-code exponent), the parametric $a$-Stern law with the $a = 2$ vanishing criterion, and an exact mod-3 carry-shift resonance, all lifted from finite integer certificates to every level $k$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Stern (1858) and Calkin-Wilf (2000) tree; Reznick (1990) binary partition functions (nearest prior art); Walsh-Hadamard transform; Thue-Morse sign; inverse Gray code. No prior record of the closed-form Gray-code Walsh spectrum, the $a = 2$ vanishing law or the mod-3 carry-shift resonance was found by the authors; the search was not recorded.

### 152. [`weak-bruhat-commutators/`](weak-bruhat-commutators/) — **77 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Running stack-sort or pop-stack-sort **before** $k$ bubble-sort passes always leaves cards at least as sorted (in the bubble-refined order) as running them **after**, for every deck and every $k$.
- **For Mathematicians:** Introduces the Bubble-Refined Order ($\preceq _ B$: value-inversion inclusion plus descent-bottom inclusion), proves $\preceq _ B$-monotonicity of $B$, and establishes all-$k$ commutators $B^k(S(xs)) \preceq _ B S(B^k(xs))$ and $B^k(P(xs)) \preceq _ B P(B^k(xs))$ with inversion and descent corollaries.
- **Prior art & stack:** Builds on Knuth (TAOCP Vol. 3, §5.2.2), West (1990), Ungar (1982), Chung–Claesson–Dukes–Graham (2010), Albert et al. (`1008.5299`), and Defant (`2104.02675`, `2104.03215`). Lean `v4.34.1` / pure `Init` (`propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`).

### 153. [`popcount-clock-borrow-accel/`](popcount-clock-borrow-accel/) — **76 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Take a whole number, count the 1s in its binary form, and flip the bit at that position; repeat. The count of 1s feeds back from the whole number to one far-away bit, yet this package proves the process is a perfect clock: one formula of the number drops by exactly one every step, so nothing ever repeats and the number turns into all 1s after a step count readable from the start. Real binary subtraction with long borrow chains behaves the same way, and on fixed-width words there is exactly one cycle everything runs into, for every width.
- **For Mathematicians:** Pure-Init Lean 4 development for the anti-causal popcount-addressed flows T_xor(x) = x XOR 2^{s_2(x)} and T_sub(x) = x - 2^{s_2(x)}: a reusable ClockCert compiler lifting pointwise finite-difference equations to the global bit-moment V(x) = 2 sum i x_i - s_2(x)^2, the exact unit clock V(T^t x) = V(x) - t with exact Mersenne hitting time V(x) + m, the synchronous-collision iff V(x) = V(y), the borrow-acceleration identity T_sub = T_xor^{1 + ctz} with Phi decreasing by exactly 1 (exact Mersenne hitting time Phi(x)), unique global attractor cycles of length 2w and w+1 for the wrapped modular flows, and the exact complement carry-borrow conjugacy.
- **Prior art & stack:** Binary inversion statistic / Young-diagram area (q-binomial theory), Hamming-weight and Thue-Morse literature, Collatz-like popcount maps. Not independently reviewed; no novelty audit has been completed, and the search behind the following claims was not recorded. Claimed: exact unit bit-moment clock and synchronous-collision criterion for the anti-causal flow x XOR 2^popcount(x), borrow-acceleration identity with exact Mersenne hitting time Phi(x), and unique 2w / (w+1)-cycle attractors for the wrapped modular flows with exact complement carry-borrow conjugacy.

### 154. [`mersenne-fermat-bitfold-crt/`](mersenne-fermat-bitfold-crt/) — **74 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Reducing a big number modulo $2^k - 1$ or $2^k + 1$ can be done by folding its bits: cut the number into $k$-bit pieces and add (or alternately subtract) them. We prove exactly when a given number of folding rounds suffices, show that the carries removed by folding are conserved regardless of the schedule, and give explicit bit patterns that split every number $2^{2^d k} - 1$ into independent residue channels.
- **For Mathematicians:** Sharp-to-the-integer Mersenne and signed-Fermat bit-folding absorption thresholds, schedule-invariant carry conservation, the minimal-depth dichotomy $d + 2$ versus $d + 1$ for $2^{2^d k}$-bit inputs (proved under $d \ge 2$, $k \ge 2$, $d \le 2^k$), the three-round Crandall threshold $c^2 \le 2^k$, and explicit Rademacher CRT idempotents for the tower $2^{2^d k} - 1$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Crandall-Fagin (1994) discrete weighted transforms and Crandall primes; Nussbaumer and Rader transforms; Kummer carry counting. The conservation law and CRT-idempotent parts are classical; the authors consider the sharp absorption thresholds, minimal-depth dichotomy, three-round Crandall threshold and Rademacher idempotents with exact popcounts less standard (no prior record found by the authors; search not recorded; not independently reviewed).

### 155. [`carry-blocks-determinants/`](carry-blocks-determinants/) — **73 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Tables counting separate carry streaks in mixed-radix addition always have determinant $\pm 1$, alongside a plateau rule where determinants ignore the top-left corner and an exact XOR bit-length formula.
- **For Mathematicians:** Division-free determinant formulas over arbitrary commutative rings $R$ for exponential and linear mixed-radix carry-blocks, binary Gray-code carry transitions, 0-based and 1-based plateau Hankel matrices, mixed-radix digit lengths, and shifted ultrametric matrices $(\mathrm{bitlen}(i \oplus j) + t) _ {2^k}$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Carry-blocks build on `carry-determinants/`; `bitlen(i ^^^ j)` sits in the classical tree-distance / ultrametric / $(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})^k$-circulant lineage (Graham & Pollak 1971; Martínez–Michon–San Martín 1994; Nabben & Varga 1994). Located related work: R. Bacher and R. Chapman, *Symmetric Pascal matrices modulo $p$*, Eur. J. Combin. 25 (2004), [arXiv:math/0212144](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0212144); R. Bacher, [arXiv:0708.1430](https://arxiv.org/abs/0708.1430); B. Sobolewski and M. Ulas, [arXiv:2607.09376](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09376) (base-2 binomial-parity and digit-sum Hankel determinants, not the exact statements above; no prior record of the Gray-code carry-transition determinant was found by that bounded audit). Lean `v4.34.1` / Mathlib (`propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`).

### 156. [`grh-ultra-compact-lean/`](grh-ultra-compact-lean/) — **71 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Compresses the already-short `grh-compact` Lean 4 formalization of the Riemann Hypothesis and Dirichlet GRH from `796` files (`76,166` lines of code, `112,854` total lines) down to **`490` files and `53,691` lines of pure code (`54,986` total lines)** without altering the theorem statements (byte-for-byte preservation and the claimed proof of RH/GRH were not independently checked here; the package is a derivative of an upstream formalization, not an original result).
- **For Mathematicians:** Source-compacted derivative of `lsernau/grh-lean` (`grh-compact`, commit `72fead10`) proving Mathlib's `RiemannHypothesis` and critical-strip zero localization $\mathrm{Re}(s) = 1/2$ for all Dirichlet $L$-functions $L(s, \chi)$ ($N \ge 1$), achieving `-38.44%` modules, `-29.51%` pure LoC, and `-51.28%` total lines with `src/Statements.lean`, `Challenge.lean`, `src/Main.lean`, and `comparator.json` preserved byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **Prior art & stack:** Strictly derived from `lsernau/grh-lean` (`grh-compact/`, Apache-2.0, Copyright 2026 Anthropic, PBC; mathematical proof by Levent Alpöge, formalized with Claude; see <https://alpo.ge/remark.pdf>). Lean `v4.35.0` / Mathlib `56a005855ea` (`propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`).

### 157. [`combinatorial-inequalities/`](combinatorial-inequalities/) — **66 / 100**
- **Plain English:** Four universal "speed limits" on stack-sorting, permutation displacement, and integer difference sets proved for every size without exception.
- **For Mathematicians:** Four universal bounds for all $n, N \in \mathbb{N}$: $3\,\mathrm{inv}(S^2(p)) \le \mathrm{pat} _ {231}(p)$ on $\mathrm{Av}(321)$, $\mathrm{des}(S(p)) \le \mathrm{peaks}(p)$, $2\,\mathrm{inv}(p) \le \mathrm{disp2}(p) + \mathrm{des}(p)(\mathrm{des}(p) + 1)$ on $\mathrm{Av}(231)$, and $3|A - A| \le 2|2A - A| + 1$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Adjacent to West (1990), Zeilberger (1992), Defant, *Stack-Sorting Preimages of Permutation Classes* ([arXiv:1809.03123](https://arxiv.org/abs/1809.03123)), Diaconis & Graham (1977), and Freiman / Ruzsa / Bukh sumset-dilate bounds. Located related work: Defant, [arXiv:2104.03215](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.03215), and Albert et al., [arXiv:1008.5299](https://arxiv.org/abs/1008.5299); no source for the exact inequalities was found by a bounded audit. Lean `v4.34.1` / pure `Std` (`propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`).

### 158. [`sorting-operators/`](sorting-operators/) — **61 / 100**
- **Plain English:** One pass of stack-sort always beats or ties one pass of bubble-sort on every list (in inversions and in descents), producing the exact same output whenever there is no 3-step decreasing chain.
- **For Mathematicians:** Constructive cross-operator comparison of West stack-sort $S$ and single-pass bubble sort $B$: $\mathrm{inv}(S) \le \mathrm{inv}(B)$, $\mathrm{des}(S) \le \mathrm{des}(B)$, $S = B$ on $\mathrm{Av}(321)$ with strictness witness `[2, 1, 3, 0]`, and $\mathrm{inv}(B) \le \mathrm{pat} _ {321} + \mathrm{pat} _ {231}$.
- **Prior art & stack:** Adjacent to West (1990), Chung–Claesson–Dukes–Graham (2010), Albert et al. ([arXiv:1008.5299](https://arxiv.org/abs/1008.5299)), and Defant (*Stack-Sorting Preimages of Permutation Classes*, [arXiv:1809.03123](https://arxiv.org/abs/1809.03123); [arXiv:2104.02675](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.02675)). Lean `v4.34.1` / pure `Init` (`propext`, `Quot.sound` only — no `Classical.choice`).

## Navigating the collection

- Use [CONTENTS.md](CONTENTS.md) for a theorem-by-theorem map across all 158 projects.
- Inside each project folder:
  - Read `README.md` for its **Synthetic Score**, **For Mathematicians** summary, **For Non-Mathematicians** plain-English overview, and prior-art citations.
  - Read `Challenge.lean` (and its small definitions module) to inspect the exact statements being proved without reading any proof code.
  - Read `Solution.lean` and `AUDIT.md` for the verification record, verbatim `#print axioms` output, and reproduction commands.

## How the results were produced

These results were explored computationally using binary arithmetic, formalized universally in Lean 4 without `sorry` or custom axioms, checked with `lake build` and `#print axioms`, and paired with Palomar `Challenge.lean` / `Solution.lean` comparator configurations.

These projects are shared as verified research artifacts. Each project's `README.md` documents the closest known prior art we are aware of. We defer to the mathematics community on matters of credit, precedence, and concurrent work—if any statement duplicates or is subsumed by an earlier result in the literature, please open an issue so we can add the citation or prune it.

## CI

Pull requests and pushes to `main` run [`.github/workflows/lean-projects.yml`](.github/workflows/lean-projects.yml) on each changed project directory. Each project passes when `lake build` succeeds on its pinned toolchain with no `sorry` outside `Challenge.lean`, and when `leanprover/comparator` with `lean4export`, the independent `nanoda` kernel, and `landrun` accepts every `comparator.json` configuration ([`.github/scripts/comparator-check.sh`](.github/scripts/comparator-check.sh)).
